Smallest amplitude with a recovered fraction of at least 0.60: pulse: 0.008, green: 0.004.

- **Headline (mechanical): with the primary signature (R 0.3, G 1.0, B 0.5) the smallest tested relative green amplitude recovered in at least 60 percent of the 432 clips is A = 0.008 (377/432 = 0.873 [0.838, 0.901]; at A = 0.004 it is 236/432 = 0.546 [0.499, 0.593], below 0.60).** With the green-only signature it is A = 0.004 (341/432 = 0.789). At A = 0.032 the fraction is 0.981 (pulse) and 0.986 (green). The fixed S-1 threshold (1.118 dB) gives the same fractions within 0.01 at every amplitude (for example 376/432 at A = 0.008).
- Prediction check (written before the run): (i) the control reproduces S-1: met (maximum difference 4.99e-6 dB and 5.0e-6 Hz on 432 clips, the S-1 file stores five decimals; fraction 0.301). (ii) the fraction rises with A: met (0.301, 0.303, 0.303, 0.343, 0.546, 0.873, 0.972, 0.981 for the primary signature). (iii) the smallest A with 0.60 lies between 0.002 and 0.016: met (0.008). (iv) above 0.9 of the recovered clips peak within 0.05 Hz of 1.2 Hz at the high amplitudes: met (367/377 at A = 0.008, 416/420 at 0.016, 424/424 at 0.032). On the unmodified clips 34/426 usable clips have their peak within 0.05 Hz of 1.2 Hz (0.080), against about 0.043 for a peak spread evenly over the 0.7 to 3.0 Hz band; this is a count, no inference is drawn from it.
- What it shows (M-005): the S-1 pipeline (YuNet face track, skin mask, POS, spectral SNR rule, shuffled null) recovers a clean 1.2 Hz sinusoid of relative amplitude 0.008 in the green channel (about one grey level at a skin value near 130) from the skin pixels of most of these clips, and does not recover one of 0.001 or less; so the S-1 failure is not caused by an insensitive pipeline in the sense that a modulation of that size and form would have passed. What it does not show: that a real pulse exists in these videos or that its amplitude, form and spectrum resemble the injected sinusoid (the amplitude of a real pulse in these videos is not measured, and no source on typical amplitudes was read); that a pulse survives H.264 compression (the injection is made after decoding and is not re-encoded); any property of forged videos. The injected phase is constant in frequency and amplitude over the clip, which is easier than a real pulse (heart rate varies). The channel weights are an implementer's choice, so the number belongs to this signature; the green-only run shows the dependence (a factor of 2 in the threshold amplitude). The S-1 verdict (FAIL, pooled 0.301) and D-052 are unchanged.

## E-014: sealed-test staircase and the three confirmatory tests   (Iteration I-8, freeze step)
- Status: PLANNED on 2026-10-25 (written before any sealed split was read, CLAUDE.md rules 2 and 3) and RUN on 2026-10-25 night after the user's go-ahead (D-067); the plan text below is kept as written, the amendment of D-062 (b) and the result follow it. Definitions: D-061 (final system), D-066 (delta 0.0100), D-025, PROTOCOL.md addendum of D-041. Scripts: scripts/freeze/ (docs/_agents/FREEZE.md).
- Date / Environment / Git commit / Seed(s): 2026-10-25 (go-ahead D-067), ENV-DESKTOP-GPU (RTX 5070, 12 GB), the freeze commit named by the tags iter-1, iter-2, iter-3, iter-4 and base (all on the same commit, the one that holds D-067; the models were logged in E-004 to E-013b and are not changed) / seeds 0, 1, 2 of every model; the heads and fine-tuned models are the ones logged in E-004 to E-013, none is changed after this entry.
- Observation: exploratory runs exist for every iteration (E-004 to E-013); no sealed split has been read. The Celeb-DF family test has 744 real and 6,906 fake videos (9.7 percent real, 44 identity groups for Celeb-DF++; D-045); the In-the-Wild test has 24,788 clips.
- Hypotheses (declared before any final run, PROTOCOL addendum): H1 (final system versus I-0, exploratory-to-confirmatory): on the sealed Celeb-DF family test the video AUC of the final system (I-4 tag, T-V from I-1) is above that of I-0, lower bound of the paired group-bootstrap difference above 0; on the sealed In-the-Wild test the audio AUC of the final system (T-A from I-4) is above that of I-0, lower bound above 0. H2 (final system versus the re-run baseline): on the sealed Celeb-DF family test, video AUC of the final system minus the Xception baseline of E-013 has a lower bound above -0.0143 (non-inferior) and, as a stronger statement, above 0 (superior).
- Predictions (sign and size; from the exploratory numbers, with the expectation that sealed values are lower than dev_x): video AUC of the final system on the Celeb-DF family between 0.75 and 0.92 against 0.60 to 0.75 for I-0; audio AUC on In-the-Wild between 0.60 and 0.85 against 0.50 to 0.70 for I-0 (the In-the-Wild test_x set contains the 12 dev_x speakers' complement, 54 minus 12 speakers; its interval is governed by the speaker groups); difference to the baseline between -0.03 and +0.05.
- Change: none to any model. For each tag I-0 to I-4, BASE, I-5, I-6, I-6b (and the label-scheme variants listed in FREEZE.md) the score file is made by scripts/freeze/score_final.py --final and evaluated once with `python -m dfd.eval.run --final --iteration <tag>`; the ledger records each (iteration, dataset); the comparisons use `--compare --final` after the regular evaluations. A second final evaluation of a tag needs `--repeat` with a reason and is listed in STATUS.md under mistakes.
- Data and split IDs: ffpp-037e2976fade test_in; celebdf_v2-82a3a70594e9 test_x and celebdf_pp-3f9df077b9eb test_x (derived datasets celebdf_family and celebdf_pp_with_v2_real, D-045); dfd-f5ff5576814a test_x; progan_test-7720c87f1b53 test_in, cnn_subset-4e973bc4da0f test_x, diffusion_test-67dd057f5042 test_x; asvspoof2019_la-875bf337c379 test_in; in_the_wild-5cf2ea54a724 test_x; lavdf_subset-e9d12decaf1f test_in (2,000 videos, exploratory).
- Success / failure lines: H1 holds for a modality if the lower bound of the paired difference is above 0; it is reported as "not shown" otherwise. H2 non-inferiority holds if the lower bound is above -0.0143; superiority if above 0; an interval that includes zero but not -0.0143 is worded "not distinguished, non-inferior by the rule of D-025". A result against the hypotheses stays in the log and in the documents. Everything outside H1 and H2 (the staircase per iteration, other datasets, LAV-DF, the image task) is exploratory and labelled so.
- Amendment of 2026-10-25 evening (D-062 b, written before any sealed read): the confirmatory tests are exactly three: (T1) T-V final system minus I-0 on `celebdf_family` test_x, (T2) T-A final system minus I-0 on `in_the_wild` test_x, (T3) final system minus the Xception baseline on `celebdf_family` test_x. The declared 95 percent rules and decisions above are unchanged. Each of the three `--compare --final` reports also prints a robustness interval at the 98.33 percent level (Bonferroni over three, 1 - 0.05 / 3), read from the same bootstrap replicates; it is reported next to the declared verdict, and a declared verdict that the 98.33 percent interval does not support (lower bound at or below 0 for "improved" or "superior", at or below -delta for "non-inferior") is written "not robust to the Bonferroni level". It does not replace the declared rule. The baseline of H2 is the E-013b baseline if 3e-4 was selected there (D-062 a, D-066), with delta recomputed in D-066. The definition of the final system is D-061: neither I-1b nor I-1c entered (E-016, E-017, D-065), so this entry's definition is unchanged. Delta of H2 is 0.0100 (D-066, the floor of the D-060 rule on the E-013b baseline), not the 0.0143 written above before the amendment; every "-0.0143" in this entry reads "-0.0100" from this amendment on.

| T3 video, final minus baseline (delta 0.0100) | celebdf_family/test_x n=7650 groups=344 | 0.8218 [0.8020, 0.8393] | 0.8042 [0.7841, 0.8223] | +0.0176 [+0.0025, +0.0327] | [-0.0011, +0.0355] | superior (and non-inferior) | not distinguished, non-inferior by the rule of D-025 |

<!-- E-014 RESULT end -->
- **Verdicts by the declared rules (mechanical): H1 video HOLDS (T1: final system minus I-0 on the sealed Celeb-DF family +0.1980 [+0.1688, +0.2242]; 98.33 percent line [+0.1627, +0.2303], robust). H1 speech HOLDS (T2: +0.1633 [+0.1177, +0.2266] on the sealed In-the-Wild test; 98.33 percent line [+0.1085, +0.2481], robust). H2 (T3, final system minus the Xception baseline at 3e-4, delta 0.0100): superior by the declared 95 percent rule (+0.0176 [+0.0025, +0.0327]); at the 98.33 percent level the interval is [-0.0011, +0.0355], so "superior" is not robust to the Bonferroni level, while non-inferiority (lower bound above -0.0100) is robust: the robust wording is "not distinguished from the baseline at the Bonferroni level, non-inferior by the rule of D-025".**
- Prediction check (written before the run; failed predictions stay): video AUC of the final system on the Celeb-DF family between 0.75 and 0.92: found 0.8218 [0.8020, 0.8393], inside. I-0 on the same set between 0.60 and 0.75: found 0.6238 [0.6015, 0.6486], inside. Audio AUC of the final system on In-the-Wild between 0.60 and 0.85: found 0.9148 [0.8896, 0.9383], ABOVE the range (the prediction failed in the optimistic direction). I-0 audio between 0.50 and 0.70: found 0.7516 [0.6802, 0.8035], ABOVE the range (failed). Difference to the baseline between -0.03 and +0.05: found +0.0176, inside. The In-the-Wild test set has 42 speaker groups (54 minus the 12 of dev_x, as written), and the speech intervals are governed by those groups.
- Reading (stated with the limits): sealed values are lower than dev_x for video (final system 0.8218 on the Celeb-DF family against 0.9096 on the development slice; Xception 0.8042 against 0.8716) and higher than dev_x for speech (final system 0.9148 against 0.8130; I-0 0.7516 against 0.6100), with the order of the staircase kept for speech (I-0 0.7516, I-2 0.8125, I-3 0.8672, I-4 0.9148) and for video (I-0 0.6238, I-1 0.8218). The video test set mixes Celeb-DF v2 test (0.8999 for I-1) and Celeb-DF++ fakes against the Celeb-DF v2 reals (the pooled set is lower, 0.7105 on `celebdf_pp_with_v2_real`), so the family number is a mixture and the 344 identity groups, not the 7,650 videos, govern its interval. Image detection and the cross-dataset image sets are exploratory (single frozen head, no change across I-0 to I-4). The LAV-DF sealed subset reproduces the development pattern: the FF++-trained visual head is below chance (I-5 visual 0.3226), the in-domain visual head separates produced from original videos (I-6 visual 0.9921, fused 0.9945) but is at chance within the produced videos (I-6bp 0.5203, I-6p visual 0.5032), the audio head detects the audio manipulation (I-6p audio 0.8480): no audio-visual detection claim (D-059). All sealed numbers other than T1 to T3 are exploratory.

## E-018: metadata-only shortcut audit on DeepAction v1   (audit of a candidate generated-video corpus, not a detector iteration)
- Status: PLANNED on 2026-10-26, written before any feature was extracted and before any model was fitted (CLAUDE.md rule 2). Autonomy tag: design chosen without the user, decided autonomously - needs user review.
- Date / Environment / Git commit / Seed(s): 2026-10-26 / ENV-DESKTOP-CPU (Windows 10 Pro 10.0.19045, Python 3.13.15 in .venv, scikit-learn 1.9.1, numpy 2.5, scipy; ffprobe 9.0.1-full_build-www.gyan.dev; versions are re-recorded by the script in e018_results.json; CUDA hidden) / the commit that holds this entry and scripts/audit/e018_metadata_audit.py / model seed 0 (the folds are deterministic, bootstrap seed 0).
- Data: data/deepaction_v1 (DATA_LOG, acquisition of 2026-10-26, G1). 2,605 mp4 files laid out as `<source>/<prompt id>/<variant>.mp4`: generators BDAnimateDiffLightning, CogVideoX5B, RunwayML, StableDiffusion, VideoPoet, Veo (generated, 2,505 files) and Pexels (real, 100 files). No pixel and no audio sample is decoded; only container and stream headers and the frame-type sequence of the first 60 video frames are read. No DeepAction content is used to train any detector, and no sealed data (DF26) is read.
- Question: can container and stream metadata alone separate real from generated clips, and the generators from each other? A yes means any content-based detector trained on this corpus could learn a shortcut instead of a forgery trace.
- Hypothesis (written before the run): H0 the metadata carry little information about the label (real-vs-generated grouped-CV AUC close to 0.5 because the authors normalised resolution to 512x512). H1 they carry a lot, because each generator and the real source have their own encoder settings (codec profile, frame rate, frame count, bit rate, GOP). Prior belief, stated for the record: H1 is more likely, since frame count and fps are fixed by each generator and differ from stock footage.
- Features, exactly (per file; ffprobe -v error -show_format -show_streams -of json, plus -show_frames on the first video stream with -read_intervals "%+#60", reading only pict_type): 1 container (format_name); 2 codec (video codec_name); 3 profile (video profile); 4 pix_fmt; 5 width; 6 height; 7 fps (avg_frame_rate evaluated as a number); 8 duration (format duration, seconds); 9 nb_frames (stream nb_frames, falling back to the decoded count from the metadata only if absent, else missing); 10 bit_rate (format bit_rate); 11 file size (bytes); 12 GOP/keyframe interval: from the first 60 frames, the mean distance in frames between I frames (if fewer than two I frames occur in 60 frames the value is 60, a censored value, plus the count of I frames in the window as a second column) and the fraction of B frames in the window; 13 audio-stream presence (number of audio streams, and presence as 0/1); 14 encoder tag (format tag encoder, else stream tag encoder, else handler_name; text, missing = "none"); 15 creation_time presence (0/1, format or stream tag); 16 color metadata (color_space, color_range, color_transfer, color_primaries, each categorical, missing = "none"). Categoricals are one-hot encoded with categories seen in the training folds only; numeric features are standardised for the logistic regression; columns that are constant over the whole corpus are reported as constant and dropped. The file name, the folder name and the prompt id are never features.
- Models: (a) logistic regression (L2, class_weight balanced, C chosen from {0.1, 1, 10}); (b) gradient-boosted trees (sklearn GradientBoostingClassifier, max_depth 2, n_estimators 100, learning_rate chosen from {0.05, 0.1}, subsample 1.0, random_state 0; class-balanced sample weights). The only tuning is the stated grid, chosen by the AUC (or log loss for the multiclass task) on 3 inner group folds inside each training set.
- Validation: groups = prompt id (the folder number, 0 to 99 for every source, so a prompt family with its variants and its Pexels clip with the same number never spans folds). Condition A: 5-fold grouped cross-validation, real (Pexels) vs generated (all six). Condition B: leave-one-generator-out for real-vs-generated: for each generator g, the same 5 group folds, training on real plus the other five generators, testing on generator g plus the real clips of the test groups. Condition C (generator identification): 7-class (six generators plus real) under the same 5 group folds, metric accuracy and balanced accuracy, chance 1/7 = 0.143 for balanced accuracy and the majority share (0.101 for a uniform generator, 100 of 2,605 real = 0.038) for plain accuracy of a constant predictor; the numeric chance level is computed by the script.
- Metric: ROC AUC (chance 0.5) computed on pooled out-of-fold scores; 95 percent interval from 2,000 bootstrap resamples of the 100 prompt-id groups (percentile). Per-generator AUC: real vs that generator, from the Condition A out-of-fold scores. Class imbalance (100 vs 2,505) is handled by balanced weights; AUC is insensitive to prior. Feature attribution: single-feature AUC (direction-free, max(AUC, 1-AUC), real vs generated, all files) for every numeric feature and every one-hot indicator, and the grouped permutation importance (drop in out-of-fold AUC when a feature group is permuted inside each test fold, 10 repeats, gradient-boosted trees).
- Success / failure lines (mechanical, fixed now): shortcut INDICATED if the lower 95 percent bound of the Condition A AUC exceeds 0.70 for either model (a shortcut exists if some simple model can exploit it); NOT INDICATED if the upper bound is below 0.60 for both models; otherwise INCONCLUSIVE. Condition B and C are reported without a verdict line and read as support: a high Condition B AUC means the cue is shared across generators, a low one means it is generator-specific.
- Prediction (sign and size): Condition A AUC above 0.95 for gradient-boosted trees; per-generator AUC high for all six; Condition C balanced accuracy above 0.80; resolution (width, height) and pix_fmt constant or nearly so. These are predictions, not results; a failed prediction stays in the log.
- Limitations (stated in advance): only 100 real videos, all from one source (Pexels), against 2,505 generated, so "real" metadata signatures are those of one stock library and its transcoding; the authors normalised resolution to 512x512, so width and height are probably constant and carry nothing here although they would in other corpora; single dataset, so nothing here transfers to other corpora; the 5 fold bootstrap over 100 groups gives wide intervals for per-generator values; the GOP window is the first 60 frames only; the audit shows what a metadata model can exploit and does not show that any particular content-based detector uses it. A positive result is a finding requiring interpretation (mitigation would be re-encoding all clips through one pipeline before training), not proof that any detector is invalid.
- Outputs: scripts/audit/e018_metadata_audit.py; data/_results/audit/e018_features.csv (raw per-file observations, one row per mp4); data/_results/audit/e018_results.json (all numbers below, environment versions). Command: `PYTHONPATH=src CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=-1 .venv/Scripts/python.exe scripts/audit/e018_metadata_audit.py`.
- Result: (to be appended after the run)
- Result (E-018, run on 2026-10-26 with scripts/audit/e018_metadata_audit.py; raw rows data/_results/audit/e018_features.csv, all numbers data/_results/audit/e018_results.json; environment: Windows-10-10.0.19045, Python 3.13.15, scikit-learn 1.9.1, numpy 2.5.4, ffprobe 9.0.1-full_build-www.gyan.dev, CPU only). 2,605 files probed (100 real, 2,505 generated; RunwayML has 505 because its folder 100 exists, VideoPoet has 400 files, Veo 100).
  - Condition A (5-fold grouped CV, groups = prompt id, real vs generated, 2000-resample group bootstrap): logistic regression AUC 1.0000 [1.0000, 1.0000]; gradient-boosted trees AUC 1.0000 [1.0000, 1.0000]. Per generator vs real: AUC 1.0000 for all six generators, both models, intervals [1.0000, 1.0000] (the lower bound of the logistic regression is 0.99999999999, a rounding artefact). By the line fixed in advance: **shortcut INDICATED** (lower bound far above 0.70).
  - Condition B (leave-one-generator-out, same folds): AUC 1.0000 [1.0000, 1.0000] for every held-out generator (BDAnimateDiffLightning, CogVideoX5B, RunwayML, StableDiffusion, VideoPoet, Veo), both models. The cue transfers to a generator never seen in training.
  - Condition C (7-class identification, real plus six generators): logistic regression accuracy 0.9996 [0.9988, 1.0000], balanced accuracy 0.9986; gradient-boosted trees accuracy 1.0000, balanced accuracy 1.0000, confusion matrix diagonal only. Chance: balanced accuracy 0.143; accuracy of a constant majority predictor 0.194.
  - Constant over the corpus (carry nothing): container (mov,mp4,m4a,3gp,3g2,mj2), codec (h264), profile (High), pix_fmt (yuv420p). Not constant, contrary to the stated expectation: width and height (prediction failed). The authors did NOT normalise to 512x512 in the files as distributed: Pexels 1080x720 to 4096x4096 (median 2160), AnimateDiff 512x512, CogVideoX 720x480, Runway 1152x640 to 1280x768, StableDiffusion 1024x576, VideoPoet 512x896, Veo 2048x1152. The limitation text above assumed normalisation; it was wrong and this line corrects it (earlier text kept).
  - Where the signal is (single-feature AUC, direction-free, real vs generated, whole corpus): has_creation_time 1.000, encoder tag "AVC Coding" (all 100 real, no generated) 1.000, colour metadata present as bt709 / tv range (all 100 real, none generated; the generated clips have no colour tags) 1.000, file size 0.985, height 0.977, bit rate 0.972, width 0.970. The real clips all carry the encoder tag "AVC Coding" and colour tags; AnimateDiff, CogVideoX, Runway and StableDiffusion carry the encoder tag Lavf58.29.100 (an ffmpeg muxer string); VideoPoet and Veo carry "Google". Per generator the frame rate and frame count are fixed constants: AnimateDiff 8 fps and 16 frames, CogVideoX 8 fps and 49 frames, StableDiffusion 7 fps and 25 frames, VideoPoet 8 fps and 81 frames, Runway 24 fps and 208 to 256 frames, Veo 30 fps (nb_frames absent from the header); the real clips range over 23.98 to 30 fps and 53 to 1500 frames. Audio: the real clips include some with an audio stream (median 0); no generated clip has audio. GOP window: 60.0 (a single keyframe in the window) is the median everywhere, so the GOP feature is weak.
  - Permutation importance (gradient-boosted trees, drop in fold AUC): 0.0000 for every feature group, because the signal is redundant: any one of several perfectly separating features (encoder tag, colour metadata, creation_time) remains after one is permuted. So importance is not informative here and the single-feature AUCs above are the attribution; no single-feature ablation was run (not claimed).
  - Prediction check (written before the run): AUC above 0.95 for gradient-boosted trees: found 1.0000, inside. Per-generator AUC high for all six: found, inside. Condition C balanced accuracy above 0.80: found 1.0000, inside. Resolution and pix_fmt constant or nearly so: found pix_fmt constant, resolution NOT constant, failed.
  - Interpretation (a finding requiring interpretation, not proof that any detector is invalid): in the files as distributed, the container and stream metadata separate the real Pexels clips from every generated clip perfectly, on prompt-disjoint folds and on an unseen generator, and identify the generator perfectly. A content-based detector trained on these files without re-encoding could therefore reach high accuracy through such cues (encoder tag, colour tags, creation time, resolution, frame rate, bit rate) instead of forgery traces; whether any given detector does so is not tested here. The separation is also an artefact risk of the corpus design (one real source, one muxing path per generator), not a property of generated video in general. Consequence for use: before any content-based evaluation on DeepAction, strip the metadata and re-encode every clip (real and generated) through one pipeline with the same resolution, frame rate, codec settings and bit-rate target, and report that the metadata audit was run (this entry). Not done in E-018. Caveats: 100 real clips from one source, one dataset, metadata of the files as stored in this download (version in DATA_LOG), first 60 frames for the GOP window, so intervals of exactly [1, 1] reflect perfect separation on this sample and not a guarantee elsewhere.

## E-015: the frozen system on the sealed identity half of FakeAVCeleb v1.2   (extra sealed set of D-062 (e); protocol D-070, Option B)
- Status: PLANNED, registered 2026-10-26 before any FakeAVCeleb clip was scored by any model. Decided autonomously within the user's instruction (Option B approved 2026-10-26) - needs user review for the details below.
- Data: FakeAVCeleb v1.2 (DATA_LOG V6; archive SHA-256 2bdf8734e81898cf7275edac62e614ee9acf68e206a46439410e3f8245cd8bff re-verified 2026-10-26, extracted to data/fakeavceleb/, 24,110 members, 6,616,868,948 bytes). Manifest data/_manifests/fakeavceleb.manifest.csv (21,544 clips). Split fakeavceleb-79d3f7051972 (src/dfd/data/fakeavceleb_split.py, salt "FakeAVCeleb-E015-2026-10-26" fixed in D-070 before computing): 10,054 clips kept, 11,490 excluded because source and target identities fall on different sides; development side dev_x 5,041 clips (250 identities), sealed side test_x 5,013 clips (250 identities). Leakage check `dfd.data.check_leakage --hash-files`: PASS; shared identities between sides: none; the 22 duplicated metadata paths differ only in the method column and are kept once with method `ambiguous:...`.
- System under test: the frozen checkpoint I-5-freeze-candidate-20261025-1608 through the adapter path of the web page (parity PASS, data/_results/freeze/parity.json). T-V: fine-tuned SigLIP base, YuNet largest face, 16 frames, temperature 0.832, threshold 0.614. T-A: mean logit of HuBERT, WavLM and Whisper-small heads, first 64,600 samples, threshold 0.695 (checkpoint metadata). Nothing is retrained, recalibrated or re-thresholded.
- Hypotheses and per-modality labels (both fixed now): H1 visual: label 1 for FVRA and FVFA clips, 0 for RVRA and RVFA (real video). H2 audio: label 1 for RVFA and FVFA, 0 for RVRA and FVRA (real audio). H3 modality independence: visual score on RVRA (0) against RVFA (1) and audio score on RVRA (0) against FVRA (1); a module tied to one modality should not separate clips that differ only in the other modality.
- Metric and uncertainty: AUC (rank-based, invariant to the temperature); group bootstrap over source identity (250 clusters), 2,000 replicates, 95 percent percentile interval, seed 20261024 (dfd.eval.bootstrap.cluster_bootstrap); sensitivity and specificity at the frozen thresholds; per-category and per-method AUC against the real class as secondary results (the 13 ambiguous-method clips are excluded from per-method rows). Clips with no detected face have no visual score: they are counted and reported, excluded from the visual AUC, and counted as "not detected" in a second sensitivity figure.
- Success and failure criteria (before the run): H1 and H2 "transfer supported" if the lower 95 percent bound of the pooled AUC exceeds 0.70; "not supported" if the upper bound is below 0.60; otherwise inconclusive. An AUC interval below 0.5 is reported as a reversal. H3 "supported" if the whole interval lies in [0.40, 0.60]; "violated" if the lower bound exceeds 0.60 or the upper bound is below 0.40; otherwise inconclusive. The predictions of the assistant (not measurements) are in data/_results/e015/predictions.json, SHA-256 77ccd957237bd0a5406928e49bf587ac1d5182bc8704058d6650dcf07ea4934a, written before scoring.
- Procedure: scripts/e015/e015.py `score --final` writes one prediction row per sealed clip (raw calibrated probabilities, no label used) to data/_results/e015/predictions_test_x.csv; its SHA-256 is recorded here before `evaluate` reads labels and computes metrics; `evaluate` appends one entry (iteration E-015, dataset fakeavceleb, split test_x) to ledger/final_ledger.jsonl and refuses a second run. The sealed side is scored once; an interrupted scoring run resumes without rescoring finished clips. No result is used for any choice. The development side is not scored here.
- Limitations known in advance: labels are video-level only; real class is 250 + 250 clips (RVRA, RVFA share the real video); source-family overlap with LAV-DF (both VoxCeleb2) and possible celebrity overlap with FF++ and Celeb-DF cannot be checked by id; the request-form terms are not in the repository (README: "released under the FakeAVCeleb Request Forms"); real audio is VoxCeleb interview audio while fake audio is clean RTVC synthesis (channel confound); Wav2Lip clips reuse the same person's face; only the first 4 s of audio is scored. A result describes this dataset and these generators only. It is not evidence about generated video (Veo, Sora, Kling, Runway).
- E-015 progress note, 2026-10-26 (appended before any label was read): sealed scoring finished with `scripts/e015/e015.py score --final`; predictions file data/_results/e015/predictions_test_x.csv, SHA-256 ac58b9f83e3b5874e7dc724e60b6969c67489c9910d0381d588b330b527cc9e5; rows, errors, visual scored, audio scored: 5013 0 5013 5013. A 6-clip smoke run on DEVELOPMENT clips (data/_results/e015/smoke_dev.csv) tested the code before the sealed run; no label was used and nothing was changed afterwards. Next: `evaluate --final` (reads labels once, appends the ledger entry).
- E-015 RESULT, 2026-10-26 (sealed half scored once; the ledger entry was appended to ledger/final_ledger.jsonl by `evaluate --final`; results file data/_results/e015/e015_results.json, SHA-256 469f21160b25ec3aacfd43232d31ea11a30cf3b1c26a2a2c743b949b11eba9ba; checkpoint I-5-freeze-candidate-20261025-1608; frozen thresholds T-V 0.614, T-A 0.695). All 5,013 sealed clips were scored, 0 errors, a face was found in every clip, audio scored in every clip. Group bootstrap over 250 source identities, 2,000 replicates, 95 percent interval.
  - H1 visual, real video (500) against fake video (4,513): AUC 0.9552 [0.9442, 0.9657]. By the pre-stated rule (lower bound above 0.70): supported. By method against the same real clips: faceswap 0.9730 [0.9548, 0.9868], fsgan 0.9871 [0.9803, 0.9928], faceswap-wav2lip 0.9908 [0.9836, 0.9962], fsgan-wav2lip 0.9895 [0.9803, 0.9960], wav2lip 0.9275 [0.9104, 0.9443]. At the frozen threshold: specificity 0.996, sensitivity 0.480 (wav2lip 0.231, fsgan 0.724, faceswap 0.933, faceswap-wav2lip 0.762, fsgan-wav2lip 0.751).
  - H2 audio, real audio (2,725) against fake audio (2,288): AUC 0.9263 [0.9149, 0.9366]: supported. RTVC-only clips (real video, fake audio) 0.9241 [0.9064, 0.9395]; fake video with fake audio 0.9266 [0.9149, 0.9373]. At the frozen threshold: sensitivity 0.962, specificity 0.637.
  - H3 independence: visual score, RVRA against RVFA (identical-content real video, different audio): AUC 0.5613 [0.5428, 0.5816], inside [0.40, 0.60]: supported, with the interval's upper part close to the edge. Audio score, RVRA against FVRA (same real audio): AUC 0.5011 [0.4801, 0.5243]: supported.
  - Predictions (data/_results/e015/predictions.json, not measurements): H1 expected 0.55 to 0.80 (observed 0.955, above the range: the prediction was wrong); H2 expected 0.40 to 0.90 (observed 0.926, slightly above the range); H3 expected 0.40 to 0.60 (both inside).
  - Interpretation (a finding, not a claim of general detection): the ranking quality is high on this dataset and the pre-registered criteria are met, but (a) the frozen thresholds do not transfer: visual sensitivity is 0.48 and audio specificity 0.64, so a user of the frozen decision rule would miss about half of the fake videos and flag about a third of the real audio; (b) the visual score separates the identical real video of RVRA and RVFA slightly (AUC 0.56), which may point to a small re-encoding or muxing effect (an inference); if so, a part of the large AUCs may come from compression differences between VoxCeleb originals and the dataset's re-encoded fakes, which this experiment cannot separate from manipulation cues; (c) the audio AUC compares clean RTVC output with VoxCeleb interview speech, a recording-channel confound that cannot be separated either (the control of H3 shows no channel difference between the real audio of RVRA and FVRA, which is a different comparison); (d) the real class is 250 identities with one clip each. The result says nothing about generated video (Veo, Sora, Kling, Runway), modern text-to-speech, segment-level localisation or other datasets. No threshold, calibration or model was changed because of it.

## E-019: canonical preprocessing protocol v1 and re-audit of technical shortcuts (DeepAction, DF26)   (preregistered 2026-10-26, before any canonical file was produced; decided autonomously - needs user review)

- Question: after every clip (real and generated, both corpora) is passed through ONE fixed re-encoding recipe, do technical shortcuts that E-018 found in the distributed files remain?
- Protocol v1 (fixed by the main agent, not tuned): ffmpeg -nostdin -i IN -map_metadata -1 -map_chapters -1 -an -sn -t 2.0 -vf "fps=7,scale=224:224:force_original_aspect_ratio=increase:flags=area,crop=224:224" -c:v libx264 -preset medium -crf 23 -pix_fmt yuv420p -g 14 -bf 2 -colorspace bt709 -color_primaries bt709 -color_trc bt709 -color_range tv -x264-params "no-info=1" -movflags +faststart OUT.mp4. No audio, first 2.0 s, 14 frames at 7 fps, 224x224 centre crop. Reasoning: DeepAction generators have fixed durations (2.0 to 10.67 s) and fps (7, 8, 24, 30); real Pexels clips vary (2.12 to 60 s, 25 or 30 fps); 7 fps is the lowest common rate, 2.0 s the shortest common duration. Clips shorter than 2.0 s are listed, not padded.
- Hypothesis: canonicalisation removes the container, encoder tag, colour tag, creation time, duration, frame count, fps, resolution and GOP shortcuts (metadata-only grouped-CV AUC falls to near 0.5), but pixel-level and temporal residues may remain, for example frame-drop jitter from fps conversion and resampling-blur differences between 1080p to 4096p real sources and 512 to 1280 px generator outputs, measurable with cheap non-semantic statistics.
- Measured: (a) E-018 metadata features recomputed on the canonical DeepAction copies, same two models (logistic regression, depth-2 gradient boosting), same grouped 5-fold CV (groups = prompt id), same inner selection, same leave-one-generator-out; AUC with 2000-resample group bootstrap. (b) Residual-cue baseline from canonical frames with OpenCV: mean and std of absolute inter-frame difference, mean and std over frames of Laplacian variance, mean luminance, mean saturation, frame count; same models, same CV, real vs generated and leave-one-generator-out; single-feature direction-free AUC for every feature. (c) DF26: descriptive per-property distributions by label and generator only (same metadata and pixel statistics). No classifier is fitted on DF26 labels, nothing is selected on DF26 (licence: held-out evaluation only).
- Success line (metadata-only grouped-CV AUC after canonicalisation, both models): shortcut REMOVED if the upper 95% bound < 0.60 for both; REMAINS if the lower bound > 0.70 for either; else INCONCLUSIVE. The same rule is applied separately to the pixel/temporal residual baseline (reported as "residual cues remain" or not).
- Failure: any clip failing to encode (listed), a protocol property still differing by label (e.g. frame count), or the metadata AUC above the REMOVED line.
- Outputs: data/_canonical/deepaction/<rel path>, data/_canonical/df26/<rel path> (git-ignored, derived data stays local); scripts/canonical/canonicalise.py, scripts/canonical/reaudit.py; data/_results/canonical/ (features CSVs, e019_results.json, failures and short-clip lists).
- Limitations: one real source (Pexels, 100 clips) in DeepAction; first 2 s only, so long-range artefacts are not seen; 224 px centre crop discards border and high-resolution traces; the pixel statistics are not semantic but are not claimed to be exhaustive; a residual baseline AUC is a finding about the corpus, not proof about any detector. DF26 results are descriptive.

## E-022: semantic validation of the SyncNet lip-sync module on controlled cases   (development set only)
- Status: PLANNED, registered 2026-10-26 before any case was run. Decided autonomously within the user's instruction - needs user review.
- Question: does `src/dfd/platform/modules/lip_sync.py` (SyncNet v2 port, placeholder thresholds MIN_CONFIDENCE 1.0, OFFSET_TOLERANCE 2 frames) behave sensibly on constructed inputs whose truth is known by construction? This is a behaviour check of the module, not an accuracy evaluation of a manipulation detector. No thresholds are calibrated for deployment by it.
- Data: LAV-DF official `train` split rows only (data/lavdf/LAV-DF/metadata.min.json, field split == "train"), with modify_video false, modify_audio false, n_fakes 0, duration at least 3.0 s. Never dev, never test. Clips selected by `random.Random(20261026)` shuffle of the eligible rows (sorted by file name first). No LAV-DF sealed part is read. Outputs of ffmpeg go to a scratch folder, not into the repository.
- Cases and N (fixed now):
  - A synchronised speech: 40 clips, unmodified. The module decides applicability itself; a clip returning not_applicable or inconclusive is reported as such, not dropped. Truth: offset near 0.
  - B shifted audio: the first 20 clips of A's list, audio shifted by -1000, -480, -240, -120, +120, +240, +480, +1000 ms (positive = audio delayed, via ffmpeg adelay; negative = audio advanced via atrim and asetpts with apad; total duration kept equal to the video). 160 files. Reference convention (observed in the smoke test, not assumed): a delay of d frames gives offset -d. Expected offset = (offset of the unshifted clip) minus (shift in frames). 1000 ms is 25 frames, outside the +-15 frame search range.
  - C ordinary variation: the same 20 clips with +-40 ms shifts (one frame), same construction. 40 files.
  - D non-speech audio over a real face video: 10 clips (the first 10 of A) each paired with 5 synthesised audio tracks of the clip's duration made with ffmpeg lavfi: white noise, pink noise, a steady 220 Hz tone, a steady 440 Hz tone, a music-like chord sequence (four chords of summed sines, 1 s each, with decay envelope). Level set so RMS is about 0.1. 50 files. Truth: no speech, so the result should be inconclusive.
  - E unsuitable inputs: E1 video without audio track (10 clips, `-an`); E2 audio with no face (10 clips, ffmpeg testsrc2 video of the same duration plus the clip's real audio); E3 under 2 s (10 clips cut to 1.5 s); E4 two faces (10 pairs of different A clips stacked side by side with hstack, audio from the left clip; the number of faces seen is not assumed); E5 audio only (5 clips, audio stream alone as .wav; passes through applicability only). 45 files. Truth: not_applicable or inconclusive; none should be consistent or potential_mismatch.
- Measures: per case the counts of not_applicable, consistent, potential_mismatch, inconclusive (and warning codes); for B and C the signed offset error in frames against the expected offset (median, mean absolute, fraction within 1 frame) restricted to completed non-inconclusive runs, plus the fraction of runs reported inconclusive; confidence distributions (min, quartiles, max) per case. Report counts with Wilson 95 percent intervals. Wall time per run.
- Hypotheses and outcome lines (fixed now):
  - H-A: on case A at most 20 percent of the clips return potential_mismatch. MATERIAL PROBLEM if more than 20 percent.
  - H-B: for the in-range shifts (+-120, +-240, +-480 ms) at least 80 percent of runs that complete have offset error within 1 frame, and those runs are potential_mismatch. For +-1000 ms (outside the range) at least 80 percent of the runs are inconclusive; MATERIAL PROBLEM if more than 20 percent of them are potential_mismatch or consistent. (The 1000 ms smoke case on one clip gave a spurious offset of 13 and potential_mismatch, so this is expected to fail with the current module.)
  - H-C: with +-40 ms shifts at most 20 percent are potential_mismatch (tolerance is 2 frames). MATERIAL PROBLEM if more than 20 percent.
  - H-D: all D runs are inconclusive. MATERIAL PROBLEM if any D run is consistent or potential_mismatch (expected to fail: the module has no speech check, SPEECH_NOT_CHECKED).
  - H-E: every E run is not_applicable or inconclusive. MATERIAL PROBLEM if any is consistent or potential_mismatch. For E4, if the detector sees a single face in fewer than 90 percent of frames the case is reported as observed and not counted against the module.
- Failure rule: a MATERIAL PROBLEM triggers a change in lip_sync.py (heuristic speech-likelihood gate from energy and spectral flatness, named a heuristic and not a speech detector; offset at the search boundary +-15 frames reported inconclusive; honest statuses). Any threshold or gate value chosen after seeing this data is marked "chosen on this development set, no held-out check done", and the post-change numbers are reported as a re-run on the same development set, not as a test. Failed runs and script errors stay in the log.
- Limitations known in advance: LAV-DF is VoxCeleb2 derived, frontal talking heads with a 224 x 224 face crop and ~4 s clips, so nothing transfers to other content; synthetic non-speech audio is not natural music or noise; one decoder path; N small; a shift by construction does not reproduce real-world desynchronisation (variable frame rate, dubbing). Script: scripts/lipsync_eval/e022.py; results: data/_results/platform/e022_lipsync_cases.json.
- Result: (to be appended after the run)

## E-021: what explains the FakeAVCeleb scores? Re-encoding, metadata shortcuts and a canonical re-encode, DEVELOPMENT half only   (follow-up to E-015)
- Status: PLANNED, registered 2026-10-26 before any dev_x clip was scored by the model and before any metadata or pixel feature was computed. Decided autonomously - needs user review. Before this entry I ran `ffprobe` by hand on 3 dev_x clips (one RVRA, its RVFA partner, one FVFA) only to learn the file layout; those probes are not measurements here, and they informed the design (stems pair as `<stem>.mp4` and `<stem>_fake.mp4` in the same folder; the RVFA partner looked re-muxed with another audio codec and a slightly different duration; the FVFA clip used a different video codec). The predictions below are therefore informed guesses, not blind ones.
- Motivation: E-015 (sealed half, final, not reopened here) gave a visual AUC of 0.5613 [0.5428, 0.5816] between RVRA and RVFA, clips that show the same real video, and warned that part of the large AUCs may be a compression or recording-channel effect. This follow-up uses the development half only (split fakeavceleb-79d3f7051972, rows labelled dev_x). No test_x clip is opened, listed or scored. Nothing is retrained, recalibrated or re-thresholded; the model is the frozen checkpoint I-5-freeze-candidate through dfd.infer.real_adapter.RealAdapter, as in scripts/e015/e015.py. Nothing found here changes the frozen system or the E-015 numbers.
- Sample (fixed seed 20261026): all dev_x RVRA clips (250) and all dev_x RVFA clips (250); a stratified random subsample of 250 FVRA and 250 FVFA clips (proportional allocation over race x gender cell within each category, numpy default_rng(20261026)). N = 1,000 clips at most (fewer only if a clip fails to decode; failures are counted and reported). Subsampled for time, since each clip is scored twice (original and canonical re-encode). The 500 real clips are the complete dev_x real class. Groups are source identities (group_id of the manifest).
- Q1 (is the RVRA vs RVFA visual gap explained by re-encoding or muxing?). Pairs: RVRA `<stem>.mp4` with RVFA `<stem>_fake.mp4` in the same folder and source id; unpaired clips are reported and excluded from paired statistics. (a) ffprobe of both groups: video codec, profile, bit rate, fps, frame count, duration, width, height, pix_fmt, container encoder tag, audio codec, audio sample rate; per group the share of each value and per pair whether video stream parameters, frame count and encoder tag are equal. (b) frozen visual score of each clip; paired difference d = score(RVFA) - score(RVRA); report median and mean of d, the share of pairs with d > 0, and the AUC of RVFA (1) against RVRA (0) with the identity-group bootstrap (2,000 replicates, seed 20261026, 95 percent percentile interval, dfd.eval.bootstrap.cluster_bootstrap). (c) the same AUC after the Q3 canonical re-encode. Prediction (not a measurement): RVRA vs RVFA AUC on dev_x is 0.50 to 0.62; most pairs differ in audio codec and encoder tag and many in frame count; after canonical re-encode the AUC moves toward 0.5. Support line (fixed now): "re-encoding or muxing explains the gap" is SUPPORTED only if (i) the pre-canonical AUC interval excludes 0.5 (lower bound above 0.50), so there is a gap to explain, (ii) in at least 80 percent of pairs the video stream parameters, frame count or encoder tag differ, and (iii) the canonical AUC interval contains 0.5 or |AUC - 0.5| falls by at least half. It is REFUTED if the canonical AUC lower bound stays above 0.50 and |AUC - 0.5| falls by less than a quarter. If (i) fails there is no gap on dev_x to explain (reported as such; the sealed gap then stays unexplained). Anything else is INCONCLUSIVE. Limit of the design: the canonical re-encode strips audio and normalises the video, but it cannot prove which pre-existing property caused a gap; a persistent gap would mean the cause lies in something that survives re-encoding.
- Q2 (shortcut potential without any semantic model). Labels: video label (RVRA, RVFA = 0; FVRA, FVFA = 1) as primary target; audio label (RVRA, FVRA = 0; RVFA, FVFA = 1) as secondary target. Features: (A) container and stream metadata from ffprobe: video codec, profile, bit rate, fps, frame count, duration, width, height, pix_fmt, container format, container size, container bit rate, encoder tag, audio codec, audio sample rate, audio bit rate, audio-minus-video duration; categorical values one-hot, numbers as is; two variants: A-video (video stream and container fields, no audio fields) and A-all. (B) cheap pixel statistics from up to 32 evenly spaced decoded frames in grayscale: mean absolute frame difference, Laplacian variance (mean over frames), a JPEG-like 8x8 blockiness (mean absolute luminance step across 8-pixel block boundaries divided by the mean step inside blocks), plus mean and std of luminance; no face, no learned weights. Classifiers: logistic regression (standardised, class_weight balanced) and sklearn GradientBoostingClassifier (depth 2, 100 trees, seed 20261026). Validation: 5-fold GroupKFold by source identity, out-of-fold probabilities, AUC with the identity-group bootstrap (2,000 replicates, seed 20261026). Feature sets: A-video, A-all, B, A-video + B. Prediction (not a measurement): A-video AUC on the video target 0.85 or above (the fake videos use other codecs); A-all on the audio target 0.95 or above (audio codec differs between real and synthetic audio); B alone 0.60 to 0.80. Fixed lines: shortcut potential is "material" if the lower bound of the best feature set exceeds 0.80 on the video target, "negligible" if its upper bound is below 0.60, otherwise "moderate". The same lines apply to pixel statistics alone. These numbers describe the shortcut available inside FakeAVCeleb, not a detector.
- Q3 (frozen visual score before and after a common canonical re-encode). Video track only: `ffmpeg -i in -an -map_metadata -1 -map_chapters -1 -vf "scale=224:224:flags=bicubic,fps=25" -c:v libx264 -crf 23 -preset medium -pix_fmt yuv420p -movflags +faststart -threads 1 out.mp4`, applied to every clip of the sample, real and fake alike; outputs stay in data/_results/e021/recoded/ (not in git). Each re-encoded clip is scored by the same adapter; only the visual score is used (no audio track remains, so no audio score is computed or reported). Task: real video (RVRA, RVFA) against fake video (FVRA, FVFA). Report AUC before and after with the identity-group bootstrap and the paired bootstrap of the difference (after minus before, same resampled identities in both, 2,000 replicates, seed 20261026), per method (faceswap, fsgan, wav2lip, faceswap-wav2lip, fsgan-wav2lip; ambiguous clips excluded from per-method rows), and the RVRA vs RVFA AUC before and after. Because the sample is subsampled, "before" is the AUC of the same sample, not the full-half number. Prediction (not a measurement): before about 0.93 to 0.97; after a drop of 0.00 to 0.10, wav2lip and the RVRA vs RVFA comparison the most affected. Fixed lines: separation is RETAINED if the lower bound of the after-AUC exceeds 0.80 and the paired-difference interval does not lie entirely below -0.05; LARGELY LOST if the upper bound of the after-AUC is below 0.70; otherwise PARTLY RETAINED. Failure of the run itself: more than 5 percent of the sample failing to decode or score in either pass; the affected numbers are then reported with the exclusions and not read as complete.
- Procedure and outputs: `PYTHONPATH=src .venv/Scripts/python.exe scripts/e021/e021.py {sample,probe,score,recode,pixels,analyse}`; outputs in data/_results/e021/ (sample.csv, probe.csv, scores_orig.csv, scores_recoded.csv, pixels.csv, e021_results.json). The script keeps only rows whose split is dev_x and aborts if any selected sample_id is not dev_x. The sealed predictions file and the results of E-015 are not read. The ledger is not touched (development data, not a final evaluation).
- Limitations known in advance: one dataset and its generators; subsample N = 1,000 of 5,041; the real class is 250 + 250 clips from 125 or so identities, so intervals are wide; the canonical re-encode is one of many normalisations and adds a lossy generation to every clip, which can hide or create cues; a metadata AUC measures shortcut availability, not that the frozen model uses it; tool versions are ffmpeg 9.0.1 and OpenCV 5.0.0 on the local machine; if the follow-up cannot separate manipulation cues from the channel, the result says so. Nothing here describes the sealed half beyond what E-015 recorded.

- Result (E-019, run 2026-10-26; scripts/canonical/canonicalise.py, reaudit.py, posthoc_no_colour_tags.py; outputs data/_results/canonical/: canonicalise_summary.json, *_failures.json, *_short_clips.json, deepaction_canonical_features.csv, df26_canonical_features.csv, e019_results.json, e019_posthoc_no_colour_tags.json; environment: Windows-10-10.0.19045, Python 3.13.15, ffmpeg 9.0.1-full_build-www.gyan.dev, OpenCV 5.0.0, CPU only; protocol v1 unchanged).
  - Encoding: DeepAction 2,605 of 2,605 clips ok, DF26 2,691 of 2,691 ok, 0 failures, 0 clips shorter than 2.0 s (nothing padded). Every canonical clip has 14 decoded frames, 7 fps, 224x224, 2.0 s, no audio, no creation time, same codec/profile/pix_fmt/encoder tag (Lavf63.1.101), colour space bt709 and range tv.
  - PROBLEM FOUND IN PROTOCOL v1 (reported, protocol not changed): the colour tags color_transfer and color_primaries are NOT normalised. They are copied from the input stream when present: all 100 Pexels reals carry bt709/bt709, all generated DeepAction clips carry none (unknown). Quick look, not a measurement: a test encode of one Veo clip with the filter setparams=colorspace=bt709:color_primaries=bt709:color_trc=bt709:range=tv appended to the -vf chain gave all four tags set; this suggests a protocol v2 (not run on the corpus). In DF26 the same leak is partial: 74 of 271 real clips carry bt709/bt709, 197 real and all 2,420 fake carry none.
  - H1 metadata-only audit on canonical DeepAction (as preregistered, both models, grouped 5-fold by prompt id): AUC 1.000 [1.000, 1.000] for logistic regression and gradient boosting, and 1.000 for every leave-one-generator-out case. Rule: REMAINS. Single-feature AUC 1.000 for color_transfer and color_primaries (the leak above); next: bit_rate and file_size 0.559 (0.559 direction-free, generated lower), gop_mean_i_dist 0.545, gop_n_i_in_60 0.544, gop_frac_b 0.521. All other E-018 features are constant (width, height, fps, duration, nb_frames, audio, creation time, container, codec, profile, pix_fmt, encoder tag, colour space and range).
  - Post-hoc, not preregistered (the two leaked colour tags removed from the feature set, same models): AUC logistic regression 0.588 [0.536, 0.640], gradient boosting 0.536 [0.478, 0.595]. Preregistered rule applied unchanged: INCONCLUSIVE (the logistic-regression upper bound 0.640 is above 0.60, lower bound below 0.70); gradient boosting alone would be below 0.60. Leave-one-generator-out post-hoc AUC 0.45 to 0.66 (logistic regression: BDAnimateDiffLightning 0.647, CogVideoX5B 0.639, RunwayML 0.577, StableDiffusion 0.451, VideoPoet 0.571, Veo 0.661). Remaining signal is bit rate / file size (a content-dependent quantity, not a header tag).
  - H2 residual pixel/temporal baseline (7 OpenCV statistics, same CV, 2000-resample group bootstrap): real vs generated AUC 0.762 [0.715, 0.807] logistic regression, 0.764 [0.719, 0.807] gradient boosting. Rule: REMAINS (lower bound 0.715 > 0.70 for both). Leave-one-generator-out (logistic regression / gradient boosting): BDAnimateDiffLightning 0.488 / 0.515 (chance), CogVideoX5B 0.776 / 0.802, RunwayML 0.778 / 0.700, StableDiffusion 0.797 / 0.725, VideoPoet 0.800 / 0.779, Veo 0.687 / 0.721. Single-feature direction-free AUC: mad_std 0.651 (generated higher), lapvar_mean 0.624 (generated lower), sat_mean 0.617 (generated higher), luma_mean 0.595 (generated higher), lapvar_std 0.578 (generated lower), mad_mean 0.564 (generated higher), n_frames_decoded constant 14 (frame count check passed for every clip).
  - DF26 (descriptive only, no classifier fitted, 2,691 canonical clips, 271 real and 2,420 fake in 10 generators): all clips 14 frames at 7 fps. Median values real vs fake: file size 22,765 vs 26,767 bytes, Laplacian variance 343 vs 409, mean frame difference 5.47 vs 6.34, saturation 83.3 vs 89.0, luminance 90.9 vs 98.3; per-generator medians differ more (for example Laplacian variance 225 for Kling_3.0 to 862 for HunyuanVideo_1.5_14b_t2v, mean frame difference 3.6 for Grok_Imagine to 9.5 for Wan_2.6), so generators differ from each other more than fakes differ from reals in aggregate. The colour-tag leak above is present (partial, real only). No verdict is given for DF26 because no classifier was fitted (licence).
  - Interpretation (a finding, not proof about any detector): canonicalisation removed the container, encoder, creation-time, duration, frame-count, fps, resolution and colour-space shortcuts, but protocol v1 as fixed leaves one perfect shortcut (two colour tags inherited from the Pexels source) so the preregistered metadata line is REMAINS; after removing those two tags post hoc the header-level residue is small and inconclusive, driven by bit rate. Cheap non-semantic pixel/temporal statistics separate real from generated at about 0.76 AUC on DeepAction, and the signal does not transfer to one generator (BDAnimateDiffLightning, chance), so it is generator-dependent. It can reflect the resampling and motion differences of the 100 real Pexels clips (one source, camera footage vs synthetic motion) as much as generation traces; this experiment cannot separate those.
  - Recommendation: canonical v1 clips are NOT yet acceptable for content-based experiments on DeepAction or DF26, because of the colour-tag leak. Run protocol v2 (v1 plus the setparams filter, or an explicit remux that rewrites the VUI tags), re-run the metadata audit (expected to drop to the post-hoc level), and then treat the remaining pixel-statistics signal (AUC about 0.76) as the corpus floor: a content-based detector on these clips should be reported alongside this baseline, and a detector that does not clearly exceed it has not shown more than low-level cues. Caveats: first 2 s only, 224 px centre crop, one real source, post-hoc analysis not preregistered, residual baseline not exhaustive.
- E-021 RESULT, 2026-10-26 (development half only; script scripts/e021/e021.py; outputs data/_results/e021/ with sample.csv, probe.csv, pixels.csv, scores_orig.csv, scores_recoded.csv, e021_results.json; ledger untouched; no test_x clip opened). Sample N = 1,000 (250 each RVRA, RVFA, FVRA, FVFA) from 250 dev identities; 0 probe failures, 0 scoring failures, 0 re-encode failures in both passes. Group bootstrap over identities, 2,000 replicates, 95 percent interval, seed 20261026. One code correction before the final analysis: the first pairing rule compared the folders RealVideo-RealAudio and RealVideo-FakeAudio and found 0 pairs; the folder name was normalised and the analysis rerun (no score changed; the preregistered rule was not touched). 249 pairs formed, 2 clips unpaired.
  - Q1. RVFA vs RVRA frozen visual AUC on dev_x: 0.5517 [0.5325, 0.5729] (gap exists; E-015 sealed: 0.5613). Paired difference RVFA minus RVRA: median 0.0022, mean 0.0099, positive in 67.9 percent of 249 pairs. Pair properties: 99.6 percent of pairs differ in video stream parameters, frame count or encoder tag (equal video stream parameters in 0.4 percent, equal frame count in 1.2 percent, equal encoder tag in 0.4 percent). Descriptives: all 250 RVRA and RVFA are h264 High yuv420p, but RVFA median video bit rate 139,781 against 179,550 for RVRA, encoder tag Lavf58.29.100 in 246 RVFA against Lavf57.83.100 in 246 RVRA, audio mp3 44.1 kHz in 246 RVFA against aac 16 kHz in 250 RVRA, median frame count 152 against 150.5. After the canonical re-encode: AUC 0.5012 [0.4806, 0.5213], paired change -0.0506 [-0.0752, -0.0278]; median paired difference 0.0001, 52.2 percent positive. By the preregistered rules the verdict is SUPPORTED: a gap exists, 99.6 percent of pairs differ (at least 80 percent), and the canonical AUC interval contains 0.5.
  - Q2 (5-fold GroupKFold by identity, out-of-fold, AUC with group bootstrap). Video target (real video vs fake video): A-video metadata gradient boosting 0.9985 [0.9961, 1.0], logistic regression 0.9894 [0.9760, 0.9993]; A-all gradient boosting 1.0000 [0.9998, 1.0]; pixel statistics alone (frame difference, Laplacian variance, blockiness, luminance mean and std) gradient boosting 0.9585 [0.9451, 0.9712], logistic regression 0.9596 [0.9457, 0.9728]. Audio target (real vs fake audio): A-all gradient boosting 0.9980 [0.9941, 1.0]; pixel statistics alone 0.5205 [0.4893, 0.5507] (chance). Shortcut potential by the fixed lines: metadata material (lower bound 0.9998 > 0.80), pixel statistics alone also material (lower bound 0.9451). The source: video codec by category is h264 for 250 of 250 RVRA and 250 of 250 RVFA but mpeg4 for 250 of 250 FVFA and 224 of 250 FVRA; the other 26 FVRA clips are h264 and all of them are faceswap (all wav2lip, fsgan, faceswap-wav2lip, fsgan-wav2lip clips in the sample are mpeg4). Audio codec: mp3 for 246 of 250 RVFA, aac for all other clips. Prediction check (not measurements): the metadata prediction (0.85 or above) was right; the pixel-statistics prediction (0.60 to 0.80) was too low (observed 0.96). Gradient boosting on the audio target also reaches 0.9974 using video-only metadata, because the video codec is correlated with fake audio in FVFA; that figure is a label-correlation effect, not an audio cue.
  - Q3. Frozen visual score on the same 1,000 clips, real video vs fake video: before 0.9732 [0.9642, 0.9816], after canonical re-encode 0.8772 [0.8562, 0.8981], paired change -0.0960 [-0.1127, -0.0803]. Fixed line: PARTLY RETAINED (after-AUC lower bound 0.8562 is above 0.80, but the paired-change interval lies entirely below -0.05, so "RETAINED" is not met). By method against the same real clips, before then after (paired change): faceswap 0.9939 then 0.9950 (+0.0012, n_fake 26); fsgan 0.9894 then 0.9518 (-0.0376 [-0.0583, -0.0197], 97); faceswap-wav2lip 0.9970 then 0.9798 (-0.0172, 61); fsgan-wav2lip 0.9965 then 0.9716 (-0.0249, 54); wav2lip 0.9548 then 0.7944 [0.7592, 0.8294] (-0.1604 [-0.1867, -0.1341], 261). Median score by category before then after: RVRA 0.011 then 0.017, RVFA 0.015 then 0.018, FVRA 0.604 then 0.275, FVFA 0.617 then 0.300 (real scores unchanged, fake scores roughly halved).
  - Interpretation. VERIFIED in the development half: (1) the RVRA vs RVFA visual gap is present (0.55) and disappears (0.50) after a common re-encode, while nearly every pair differs in encoder tag, bit rate and frame count, so for this pair the gap is consistent with a re-encoding effect; the design cannot exclude every other pixel-level difference, and it does not show which property (bit rate, frame count, encoder generation) carries it. (2) FakeAVCeleb contains a very large technical shortcut: codec alone (mpeg4 for fake video, h264 for real video, except 26 faceswap clips) separates real from fake video at AUC about 0.99 or above, audio codec nearly separates the real-audio-fake-audio RVFA clips, and cheap pixel statistics reach 0.96 on the video target without any face or learned feature. (3) The frozen visual score is partly tied to these properties: after a common re-encode the pooled AUC falls from 0.973 to 0.877 and the wav2lip AUC from 0.955 to 0.794, with fake scores falling and real scores unchanged. NOT RESOLVED: whether the remaining 0.877 (and 0.794 for wav2lip) is manipulation evidence or a residue of the mpeg4 first generation that survives one more lossy encode (double-compression traces); the canonical re-encode is applied after the fakes were already mpeg4, so it cannot produce a codec-matched control. Whether the E-015 sealed AUC of 0.955 is similarly inflated is a hypothesis that follows from the development result and is not tested on the sealed half. The sealed numbers stand as recorded; this result says they must be read as ranking quality on a dataset with a codec shortcut, not as evidence of detecting face manipulation per se. Not claimed: any effect of the shortcut on the model's weights, any generalisation beyond FakeAVCeleb, any statement about the E-015 sealed half beyond what E-015 recorded. Next options (not done, need a decision): a codec-matched control (re-encode the real clips to mpeg4 at matched bit rate before scoring), which would test the residue hypothesis directly. Tag: decided autonomously - needs user review.
- E-022 RESULT, 2026-10-26 (script scripts/lipsync_eval/e022.py; results data/_results/platform/e022_lipsync_cases_before.json (module syncnet-v2-port-1, MIN_CONFIDENCE 1.0) and e022_lipsync_cases_after.json (syncnet-v2-port-2); device cuda RTX 5070; 0 ffmpeg failures, 0 script errors; the names in the Script line above differ: two files with _before and _after suffixes were written). Same 40 LAV-DF train clips (seed 20261026), same media, N as preregistered (A 40, B 160, C 40, D 50, E 45). Offset sign: delaying audio by d frames gives offset -d, as in the smoke test.
  - BEFORE (port-1). A: 35 consistent, 5 potential_mismatch (12.5 percent, [5.5, 26.1] Wilson; those 5 clips had confident non-zero offsets of -3, -3, -3, -4, -9 frames at confidence 6.4 to 7.5, i.e. the unmodified files themselves measure off-sync, not a shift we made). H-A passed. B in-range shifts: offset error against expected (unshifted offset minus shift) had median 0 and 100 percent within 1 frame in all 6 in-range shifts (20 runs each); finding potential_mismatch in 11/20 (-120 ms) to 20/20 (+-240, +-480) because a 120 ms shift is 3 frames and the clips already have their own offsets within tolerance. 1000 ms: 14 and 12 of 20 potential_mismatch, 1 and 2 consistent, only 5 and 6 inconclusive, with random offsets (median absolute error 19 and 36.5 frames): H-B FAILED, material problem. Confidence at 1000 ms was 0.46 to 2.66 against 4.46 to 9.72 for in-range runs. C: -40 ms 1/20 and +40 ms 5/20 potential_mismatch (15 percent pooled, [7.1, 29.1]); pooled by the preregistered rule not material, but +40 ms alone is 25 percent and is made of the clips whose own offsets were already near the tolerance edge (inference). D: 49 of 50 inconclusive (only by LOW_SYNC_CONFIDENCE, confidence 0.06 to 1.07), 1 of 50 (chord sequence, confidence 1.07) potential_mismatch at offset 12: H-D FAILED, material problem. E: E1 no audio 10/10 not_applicable, E2 no face (testsrc2) 10/10 not_applicable, E3 under 2 s 10/10 inconclusive (CLIP_TOO_SHORT), E4 hstack of two clips 10/10 inconclusive (SPEAKER_ATTRIBUTION_NOT_ESTABLISHED), E5 audio only 5/5 not_applicable: H-E passed.
  - CHANGES made because of the material problems (module lip_sync.py, version syncnet-v2-port-2; tests/platform_lipsync 19 passed): (1) MIN_CONFIDENCE 1.0 to 3.0, chosen by inspecting the gap on this development set (out-of-range <= 2.66, in-range >= 4.46); (2) a heuristic speech-likelihood gate (median spectral flatness of active 25 ms frames in [0.001, 0.28] and RMS coefficient of variation >= 0.15), named a heuristic and not a speech detector, giving inconclusive AUDIO_NOT_SPEECH_LIKE; its numbers were set from feature ranges of the 40 speech tracks (flatness 0.003 to 0.202, cv 0.243 to 0.964) against the 50 synthetic tracks (flatness 0.0 for tones and chords, 0.34 to 0.57 for noise); (3) an offset at the search boundary (+-15) is now inconclusive OFFSET_AT_SEARCH_BOUNDARY. The weights licence note is unchanged ("weights licence not stated by the source"). Thresholds (1) and (2) are CHOSEN ON THIS DEVELOPMENT SET; NO HELD-OUT CHECK WAS DONE, so the AFTER numbers are a re-run on the same data, not a test, and say nothing about generalisation.
  - AFTER (port-2, same media). A unchanged (35 / 5). B 1000 ms: 20/20 inconclusive in both directions (LOW_SYNC_CONFIDENCE); in-range shifts unchanged except one +480 ms run now inconclusive (offset at the boundary); offset errors still median 0, 100 percent within 1 frame. C unchanged. D: 50/50 inconclusive (AUDIO_NOT_SPEECH_LIKE). E unchanged. By construction the AFTER pass of H-B and H-D follows from thresholds fitted to the same cases.
  - Remains uncalibrated or untested: tolerance of 2 frames (some unmodified LAV-DF clips measure -3 to -9 frames, so a larger real-world false-alarm rate is likely on other content); confidence minimum and speech heuristic on natural music, singing, speech over noise, other languages, low-quality or non-frontal faces; false-alarm rate on real data other than LAV-DF train; sensitivity on actual lip-sync fakes (not run); the clips are 4 s long. A potential_mismatch is never proof of manipulation.

## E-020: three generated-video candidates on DeepAction (development) and DF26 (held-out evaluation): C1 ReStraV-style trajectory MLP, C2 logistic regression on the mean DINOv2-small CLS embedding (the head that was installed), C3 pixel statistics   (registered 2026-10-26 in this document; see docs/RESEARCH_INTEGRITY.md for what git can and cannot show about the ordering; decided autonomously - needs user review)
- Status: COMPLETED 2026-10-26 (development RESULT, DF26 RESULT, diagnostic and reproducibility check appended below; this line was "PLANNED" when the entry was registered and was updated after the audit of 2026-10-26, nothing else in the entry was changed). Naming clarification (post-audit): the heading originally said "ReStraV-style generated-video detector", which describes only candidate C1; the head that is installed in the platform is C2, a logistic regression on the mean CLS embedding of frozen DINOv2-small, an appearance baseline and NOT the ReStraV method. Gate: it runs only if the E-019 v2 addendum reports that the header-metadata shortcut is removed (its preregistered rule); otherwise E-020 is not run and the generated_video module stays `unsupported`.
- Purpose: build the smallest defensible generated-video detector from assets already on disk and measure honestly whether it separates generated from camera-captured video on generators it has not seen (DeepAction leave-one-generator-out) and on a different, modern talking-head benchmark (DF26, evaluation only).
- Data and licences: DeepAction v1 (DATA_LOG G1; generated folders CC BY 4.0, real folder Pexels licence) after canonical protocol v2 (E-019): 2,605 clips, 100 real. DF26 (DATA_LOG G2, LICENSE.md v1.0): used for held-out evaluation ONLY; it is never read before the heads are frozen and nothing from it is used to train, tune, select, calibrate or set a threshold (the licence prohibits all of that outside the open-generator LOGO exception, which this experiment does not use). DF26 clips: 2,691 (271 real, 2,420 generated; closed and open generators by metadata/generators.csv).
- Method (fixed now): canonical-v2 clip (first 2.0 s, 7 fps, 14 frames, 224 px, colour tags set to bt709/tv); 8 frames at indices 0 2 4 6 7 9 11 13 through frozen DINOv2-small (IMG-07, hash-verified, fp32, last_hidden_state). Candidates: C1 = the 21 trajectory values of the ReStraV description (7 distances, 6 angles, mean/variance/min/max of each) into a standardised MLP (64, 32), ReLU, Adam, alpha 1e-3, max_iter 500, reals replicated to class balance; C2 = control: logistic regression (C = 1, balanced) on the mean CLS embedding of the 8 frames (appearance only, no temporal geometry); C3 = shortcut control: logistic regression on 7 cheap pixel statistics (mean and std of frame difference, mean and std of Laplacian variance, mean luminance, mean saturation, mean per-frame luminance std). Seeds 0, 1, 2 for C1 (C2 and C3 are deterministic).
- Development protocol (DeepAction only): 5 folds of prompt-id groups (the folder number 0 to 99; seeded shuffle, seed 0), so a prompt family and its real counterpart never span train and test. Leave-one-generator-out: for each of the six generators g and each fold f, train on clips of the other five generators and the reals outside fold f, score the clips of g and the reals of fold f. Primary metric: macro mean over the six generators of AUC(real vs g) on the pooled out-of-fold scores, with a 2,000-resample cluster bootstrap over prompt groups (95 percent percentile interval; same resamples for paired differences; seed 20261026) and the seed standard deviation for C1.
- Selection rule (fixed now): a candidate is eligible if its macro AUC lower bound exceeds 0.70 AND its paired difference to C3 has a lower bound above 0 (C3 is the shortcut control and is never deployed). Choose the eligible candidate with the highest point estimate; if C1 is chosen and its paired difference to C2 has a lower bound at or below 0, choose the simpler C2. If none is eligible, no head is deployed and the result is a negative finding. Threshold of the chosen candidate: maximum balanced accuracy on the pooled out-of-fold scores (each generator against the reals scored for it). Final heads (C1, C2, C3 as comparators) are trained on all 2,605 DeepAction clips with seed 0 and hashed BEFORE any DF26 file is read.
- Held-out evaluation (once, `scripts/genvideo/e020.py df26 --final`, ledger entry iteration E-020, dataset df26): all three frozen heads score all 2,691 DF26 canonical clips. Primary: AUC of the chosen head on closed-generator fakes against the 271 reals, cluster bootstrap over source groups (paired_real_path), 2,000 resamples. H-G1 supported if the lower bound exceeds 0.70; not supported if the upper bound is below 0.60; otherwise inconclusive. Secondary: AUC per generator, open subset, sensitivity and specificity at the fixed development threshold, C2 and C3 for comparison. No result is used for any choice; the module's tested_scope text will quote the DF26 result as measured.
- Predictions (not measurements): C1 macro LOGO AUC 0.65 to 0.85; C3 0.55 to 0.75 (v1 residual baseline reached 0.76 pooled but about 0.5 on AnimateDiff); C1 beats C3 only modestly; DF26 closed-subset AUC of the chosen head 0.50 to 0.65 (talking-head content, 720p sources, I2V generators are far from the action-video training set), so H-G1 is more likely not supported or inconclusive than supported.
- Exclusions: clips failing the canonical re-encode or the 14-frame check (none expected; listed if any). DF26 clips used only as evaluation inputs.
- Safeguards: canonical-v2 pixels path is identical for training, evaluation and live use (src/dfd/platform/genvideo/canonical.py); header metadata cannot reach the model (features come from pixels only); C3 measures what cheap pixel statistics alone reach; E-018 and E-019 are preserved; no frozen face, speech or image checkpoint is touched; heads live in checkpoints/genvideo-E020/ (new directory).
- Limitations known in advance: 100 real clips from one source (Pexels) against 2,505 generated; first 2 s only; 224 px centre crop; DeepAction content is action videos, DF26 is talking heads; the method is an adaptation of ReStraV (8 of 14 frames, our own classifier and data, last_hidden_state tokens flattened as an assumption); a high score is a statement about these corpora and not about Sora, Kling or any generator absent from them.

## E-024: independent validation of the frozen lip-sync module (syncnet-v2-port-2) on fresh clips and a second source   (development data only)
- Status: PLANNED, registered 2026-10-26 before any E-024 clip was built or scored. Decided autonomously - needs user review.
- Why: E-022 chose MIN_CONFIDENCE 3.0 and the speech-likelihood values on the same 40 LAV-DF train clips that it then used to measure the module, so its AFTER numbers are exploratory. E-024 measures the FROZEN module on data that did not take part in choosing any threshold. Nothing in lip_sync.py, the syncnet package or any threshold is changed by this experiment; if a line fails it is reported as failed and the module is not retuned and re-reported as validation. Any later change needs a new preregistered experiment on yet other clips.
- Frozen configuration: module version syncnet-v2-port-2, MIN_CONFIDENCE 3.0, OFFSET_TOLERANCE_FRAMES 2, MIN_SECONDS 2.0, speech gate flatness [0.001, 0.28] and RMS cv >= 0.15, offset at +-15 reported inconclusive, YuNet face detector, device cuda. The weights-licence note is unchanged and unresolved ("weights licence not stated by the source").
- EXCLUDED clips (those used to SET thresholds in E-022): the 40 LAV-DF train clips returned by pick_clips() in scripts/lipsync_eval/e022.py: rows of data/lavdf/LAV-DF/metadata.min.json with split train, modify_video false, modify_audio false, n_fakes 0, duration >= 3.0, sorted by file name, shuffled with random.Random(20261026), first 40. Their ids are the "clips" field of data/_results/platform/e022_lipsync_cases_before.json (identical in _after.json). e024.py recomputes that list with the same rule, asserts equality with the JSON, writes it to data/_results/platform/e024_excluded_e022_clips.json and removes it from every E-024 pool. Only train rows are used; no LAV-DF dev or test row is read; no FakeAVCeleb test_x clip is read. New clips and newly built synthetic audio are used for every case.
- Fresh data (fixed seed 20261027 for every draw; media built with ffmpeg in a scratch folder outside git; FakeAVCeleb clips are read in place):
  - (a) LAV-DF synchronised speech: same eligibility as E-022 minus the 40 excluded; sorted file names shuffled with random.Random(20261027); N = 60 clips. Truth: offset near 0 (an unmodified file may carry its own offset, as E-022 found for 5 of 40).
  - (b) FakeAVCeleb synchronised speech, a second independent source: split data/_splits/fakeavceleb-79d3f7051972.csv, rows dev_x only, category RVRA (real video, real audio); one clip per source identity (group_id); random.Random(20261027); N = 60.
  - (c) Known lip-sync fakes, dev_x only, one clip per identity within each stratum, N = 40 per stratum: FVRA/wav2lip (video lip-synced to the real audio), FVFA/wav2lip, FVFA/faceswap-wav2lip, FVFA/fsgan-wav2lip (video lip-synced to synthetic audio). Measure: fraction potential_mismatch / inconclusive / consistent / not_applicable per stratum with Wilson 95 percent intervals. PREDICTION, not a success line: a SyncNet-style measure is expected NOT to flag Wav2Lip clips (a low potential_mismatch rate), because Wav2Lip is trained against a lip-sync expert discriminator that is itself a SyncNet-like network, so its output is tuned to look synchronised to such a measure. A low flag rate is therefore a prediction and not a failure of the module; the module limitations already state that a synchronised fake passes. A "consistent" verdict on a fake does not show authenticity. No pass or fail is assigned to (c); the comparison with (b) is descriptive.
  - (d) Deliberate desynchronisation of the first 30 clips of (a): audio shifted by -1000, -480, -240, -120, +120, +240, +480, +1000 ms, same ffmpeg construction as E-022 (positive = audio delayed by adelay; negative = audio advanced by atrim and asetpts with apad; video stream copied; duration kept). 240 files. In range: +-120, +-240, +-480 ms (3, 6, 12 frames); out of range: +-1000 ms (25 frames, beyond the +-15 search). Offset error = measured offset minus expected offset, expected = (offset of the unshifted clip) minus (shift in frames), the sign convention E-022 observed (a delay of d frames gives offset -d). Offset error exists only where the unshifted clip returned a number; the denominator for the lines below is all shifted files whose unshifted clip was completed and not inconclusive.
  - (e) Ordinary variation: the same 30 clips shifted by -40 and +40 ms; 60 files.
  - (f) Non-speech audio over real face video: the first 20 clips of (a), each with 5 synthesised ffmpeg lavfi tracks (white noise, pink noise, 220 Hz tone, 440 Hz tone, four-chord decaying sequence; same recipes as E-022); 100 files. Natural music, singing, ambient sound and speech over noise are NOT tested: I checked data/ (asvspoof2019_la and in_the_wild are speech; no music or ambient corpus is present), so anything about natural music is untested.
  - (g) Unsuitable inputs from clips 31 to 50 of (a): (g1) video without audio (-an), 20; (g2) no face: ffmpeg testsrc2 video plus the clip real audio, 20; (g3) shorter than 2 s: cut to 1.5 s, 20; (g4) audio-only .wav through the applicability check, 10. 70 files.
- Success lines (fixed now; the point estimate is judged, the Wilson 95 percent bound is reported, per source):
  - L-A and L-B (synchronised speech, (a) and (b) separately): potential_mismatch rate at most 10 percent; if the upper Wilson bound exceeds 20 percent the pass is called weak. Inconclusive and not_applicable rates are reported; an inconclusive rate above 30 percent on synchronised speech is flagged as a usability failure.
  - L-D-in (in-range shifts pooled): at least 90 percent of shifted files have |offset error| <= 2 frames OR are flagged potential_mismatch. A stricter reading (|offset error| <= 2 frames among completed non-inconclusive runs) and the inconclusive share are reported beside it, per shift. L-D-480: the false "consistent" rate for +-480 ms (12 frames, far outside the 2 frame tolerance) at most 10 percent.
  - L-D-out (+-1000 ms): consistent in at most 10 percent; the expected outcome is inconclusive; potential_mismatch with a wrong offset is reported as a concern but does not by itself fail the line.
  - L-E (+-40 ms): potential_mismatch at most 20 percent (informational; E-022 found 15 percent).
  - L-F (synthetic non-speech): at most 5 percent of files non-inconclusive (consistent or potential_mismatch).
  - L-G (unsuitable): 100 percent not_applicable or inconclusive.
  - Overall: the frozen module is "validated on this protocol" only if every line passes. A failed line is reported as failed with its number; nothing is repaired in this experiment.
- Limitations known in advance: LAV-DF and FakeAVCeleb are both VoxCeleb2 derived (frontal celebrity interviews, mostly English), so the two sources are different pipelines but not different domains, and E-022 and E-024 may share speakers (independence from E-022 is by clip, not by speaker; not checked); synthetic non-speech is not natural music; shifts by construction do not reproduce real dubbing or variable frame rate; small N, wide intervals; unmodified clips may carry their own offsets; the clips are scored once and nothing is tuned. Script scripts/lipsync_eval/e024.py; results data/_results/platform/e024_lipsync_validation.json.
- Result: (to be appended after the run)

## E-023: codec-matched control for the FakeAVCeleb visual score, DEVELOPMENT half only   (follow-up to E-021)
- Status: PLANNED, registered 2026-10-26 before any E-023 clip was scored by the model. Decided autonomously - needs user review.
