Script scripts/e023/e023.py (parameter measurement in scripts/e023/measure.py, run before this entry; it read ffprobe metadata and frame types only, no model, and wrote data/_results/e023/measure.json and fvra_all_probe.csv). No test_x clip is opened, listed or scored; data/_results/e015 and freeze are not read. Frozen model only: dfd.infer.real_adapter.RealAdapter on checkpoints/I-5-freeze-candidate; no retraining, recalibration or threshold change.
- Question left open by E-021: the frozen visual AUC real video vs fake video is 0.973 on original files and 0.877 after a common canonical re-encode. Is the residual 0.877 manipulation evidence, or a leftover trace of the fakes' earlier mpeg4 compression (codec confound: real clips are h264, fake clips mpeg4)?
- Measured recipe (dev FVRA mpeg4 clips of the E-021 sample, N = 224, ffprobe): codec mpeg4, Simple Profile in 224 of 224; pix_fmt yuv420p; container mov/mp4; frame rate 25 in 222 (30 and 23.976 in one each); no B frames in any clip; keyframe gap exactly 12 frames in 224 of 224 (GOP 12); video bit rate quartiles 280,840 / 317,957 (median) / 433,385 bit/s; encoder tag Lavf58.45.100 (127) or Lavf58.12.100 (97); size 224x224 in 222 clips. For reference, the real RVRA clips are h264 at median 179,550 bit/s. Measured over ALL 2,518 dev FVRA clips (metadata only): faceswap 272 h264; fsgan 957 mpeg4; wav2lip 1,289 mpeg4. So the h264 fakes are exactly the faceswap clips and codec is completely confounded with method inside FVRA (no mpeg4 faceswap and no h264 fsgan or wav2lip clip exists in the dev half).
- Recipe M (main), applied to the original clip, video only: `ffmpeg -i in -an -map_metadata -1 -map_chapters -1 -vf fps=25 -c:v mpeg4 -b:v 317957 -g 12 -bf 0 -pix_fmt yuv420p -threads 1 mid.mp4`; the achieved bit rate of every mid file is measured and reported. Recipe L (sensitivity, heavier compression of the real clips): same with -b:v 180000. Then the E-021 canonical re-encode, command unchanged, on mid.mp4. Limits stated now: the recipe copies measured stream parameters, not the unknown generator pipeline (the fakes were written by the dataset authors' tools, probably from decoded face crops); real clips go h264 to mpeg4 to h264, fakes go (unknown) to mpeg4 to h264, so the real clips carry one more generation than the fakes' recorded history in the h264 source; matching is therefore approximate.
- Data. Real: the 250 dev RVRA clips of the E-021 sample. Fake: the 500 dev fake clips (250 FVRA, 250 FVFA) of the E-021 sample. Groups = source identity; paired bootstrap over identities (union of real and fake identities resampled together), 2,000 replicates, seed 20261026, 95 percent percentile interval. Existing frozen scores of E-021 (scores_recoded.csv) are reused for the unmatched canonical baseline; new scoring: C1 = RVRA through M then canonical (250), C1L = RVRA through L then canonical (250), C2 = the 500 fakes through M then canonical (reverse control). Codec-split set: all 272 dev FVRA h264 (faceswap) clips (set H) and 272 dev FVRA mpeg4 clips drawn at random with numpy default_rng(20261026) from the 2,246 mpeg4 dev FVRA clips (set K); both scored on original files and after canonical re-encode (about 544 x 2 scorings). N total about 2,100 scorings plus the 1,000 existing.
- Quantities. B = AUC(RVRA canonical vs the 500 canonical fakes) from E-021 scores (baseline, about 0.88). P1 = AUC(C1 RVRA vs the same 500 canonical fakes): the codec-matched AUC (PRIMARY). S1 = same with C1L. R1 = AUC(RVRA canonical vs C2 fakes), reverse control. Paired differences P1 - B, S1 - B, R1 - B. Codec split: A_H = AUC(RVRA vs H) and A_K = AUC(RVRA vs K), each original and canonical and against C1 reals; difference A_H - A_K (same reals, bootstrap over identities); plus per-method AUC of the 500-fake sample for context.
- Outcome lines (fixed now, applied to P1 with its 95 percent interval). (i) "The codec shortcut explains the residual": upper bound of P1 below 0.60. Would show: once the real clips carry an mpeg4 history the frozen visual score no longer separates them from fakes beyond chance-like levels, so the 0.877 was mostly a codec-history cue. Would not show: that the model is useless on real manipulations, or that the real-world codec history of fakes is reproduced. (ii) "Manipulation evidence remains": lower bound of P1 above 0.70. Would show: separation survives after the codec history is equalised by this recipe. Would not show: absence of any other channel difference (bit-rate, resolution history, generator-specific noise, content and identity differences), nor generalisation outside FakeAVCeleb, nor anything about the sealed half. (iii) otherwise INCONCLUSIVE. The same lines are applied to S1 (sensitivity) and reported; if P1 and S1 fall in different bands the verdict is INCONCLUSIVE. Reverse control reading (descriptive): if R1 - B is positive with an interval above 0, extra mpeg4 history in fakes raises separation, which supports a compression-history sensitivity; if it is about 0 or negative this evidence is weak or absent. Codec split reading: if canonical A_H lower bound exceeds 0.70 the separation of fakes with no mpeg4 history (faceswap h264 only) survives the canonical re-encode, which argues against a pure mpeg4-residue explanation for that method; if A_H - A_K interval excludes 0 the score depends on the fake's codec or on its method (inseparable here, because they coincide); this split cannot separate codec from method, so it is not used for the main verdict.
- Prediction (not a measurement): P1 between 0.70 and 0.85 (a partial drop from 0.877); R1 within 0.03 of B; A_H canonical above 0.90 (E-021 had 0.995 on 26 clips). Failure of the run: more than 5 percent of any set failing to transcode or score; affected numbers then reported with exclusions.
- Not claimed in advance: any amount by which the E-015 sealed AUC is inflated. Whatever is found describes dev_x only.

## E-019 v2 addendum: canonical protocol v2 (colour tags forced) and re-audit   (preregistered 2026-10-26, before any v2 file was produced; decided autonomously - needs user review)
- Honesty note: the split of audit features into SET H and SET S below was decided AFTER seeing the v1 results (E-019 RESULT: perfect colour-tag shortcut, residual signal in bit rate and file size). It is a post-v1 design choice, not a blind one. The acceptance numbers were fixed here, before v2 outputs exist, and are not changed afterwards.
- Protocol v2 = v1 plus `setparams=colorspace=bt709:color_primaries=bt709:color_trc=bt709:range=tv` appended to the -vf chain, exactly as defined in src/dfd/platform/genvideo/canonical.py (ffmpeg_args, one definition, imported by scripts/canonical/canonicalise_v2.py). Everything else equals v1 (first 2.0 s, 7 fps, 224x224 crop, libx264 medium crf 23, yuv420p, g 14, bf 2, no audio, no metadata). v1 scripts and v1 outputs (data/_canonical, data/_results/canonical) are preserved untouched.
- Outputs (git-ignored): data/_canonical_v2/{deepaction,df26}/<same relative path>, data/_results/canonical_v2/. Scripts: scripts/canonical/canonicalise_v2.py, scripts/canonical/reaudit_v2.py (imports reaudit.py and the E-018 functions; those files are not modified). Clips shorter than 2.0 s are listed, not padded; failures are listed.
- Feature sets (declared now). SET H (header-only): container, codec, profile, pix_fmt, color_space, color_range, color_transfer, color_primaries, encoder_tag (categorical); width, height, fps, nb_frames, duration, has_creation_time, has_audio, n_audio_streams, n_frames_probed, gop_mean_i_dist, gop_n_i_in_60, gop_frac_b (numeric). SET S (content-complexity proxies): bit_rate, file_size. Reason: at fixed CRF, bit rate and file size measure how hard the content is to compress, not container metadata.
- Audit (DeepAction v2, 2,605 clips): grouped 5-fold CV, groups = prompt id, logistic regression and depth-2 gradient boosting (E-018 implementations), 2,000-resample group bootstrap, seed 0 as in E-018/E-019. Run on SET H, on SET S, on SET H + S, and the v1 pixel/temporal residual baseline (n_frames_decoded, mad_mean, mad_std, lapvar_mean, lapvar_std, luma_mean, sat_mean) with the v1 rule (REMAINS if either model has CI lower bound > 0.70, REMOVED if both upper bounds < 0.60, else INCONCLUSIVE; reported as residual cues remain or not). Single-feature direction-free AUCs for all SET H features. Verification: ffprobe of the four colour tags (color_space, color_primaries, color_transfer, color_range) on a random sample of at least 50 clips per source type (real, generated) in both corpora.
- ACCEPTANCE for "header shortcut removed" (fixed now): (a) SET H metadata-only grouped-CV AUC upper 95 percent bound below 0.60 for BOTH models AND (b) every SET H feature is constant across labels or has direction-free single-feature AUC below 0.60. Both needed. SET S is reported separately as a documented content-complexity proxy and is not part of the acceptance rule; the pixel residual baseline is reported, not part of the rule either. If acceptance fails, results are kept, the failing feature is named, and a v3 is proposed, not applied.
- DF26: descriptive only (no classifier fitted on DF26 labels): the same features by label and by generator, including colour tags by label.
- Prediction (not a measurement): colour tags become constant; SET H AUC about 0.5 with upper bound below 0.60; SET S 0.54 to 0.60 as in the v1 post-hoc; pixel residual baseline about 0.76 as in v1.
- Result: (to be appended after the run)
- E-019 v2 RESULT, 2026-10-26 (scripts/canonical/canonicalise_v2.py, reaudit_v2.py; outputs data/_canonical_v2/, data/_results/canonical_v2/: canonicalise_summary.json, deepaction_canonical_v2_features.csv, df26_canonical_v2_features.csv, e019_v2_results.json; Windows-10-10.0.19045, Python 3.13.15, ffmpeg 9.0.1-full_build-www.gyan.dev, OpenCV 5.0.0, CPU only; v1 files untouched). Acceptance rule applied exactly as fixed above. Tag: decided autonomously - needs user review.
  - Encoding: DeepAction 2,605 of 2,605 ok, DF26 2,691 of 2,691 ok, 0 failures, 0 clips under 2.0 s, 0 unknown durations. Every clip has 14 decoded frames (100 of 100 real DeepAction checked).
  - Colour tags verified with ffprobe on a random sample of 60 clips per group (seed 20261026): DeepAction real, DeepAction generated, DF26 real, DF26 fake: 240 of 240 have color_space, color_primaries, color_transfer all bt709 and color_range tv. Over all clips the audit features show color_transfer and color_primaries constant (bt709) in both corpora; DF26 by label: real 271 bt709, fake 2,420 bt709. The v1 leak is closed.
  - SET H (DeepAction, grouped 5-fold by prompt id, 2,000-resample group bootstrap): logistic regression AUC 0.518 [0.472, 0.558]; gradient boosting 0.555 [0.507, 0.605]. Constant across clips: width, height, fps, duration, nb_frames, audio fields, has_creation_time, n_frames_probed, container, codec, profile, pix_fmt, encoder_tag and all four colour tags. Non-constant: gop_mean_i_dist (single-feature direction-free AUC 0.544), gop_n_i_in_60 (0.544), gop_frac_b (0.526); all below 0.60. Part (b) of the acceptance rule PASSES.
  - ACCEPTANCE: part (a) FAILS by a small margin: the gradient-boosting upper bound is 0.6048, not below 0.60 (logistic regression 0.558 passes). Therefore "header shortcut removed" is NOT met by the preregistered rule (verdict from the v1 line: INCONCLUSIVE). Feature responsible: the only non-constant SET H features are the three GOP statistics (gop_mean_i_dist, gop_n_i_in_60, gop_frac_b); they are not container tags but they come from x264 scene-cut keyframe and B-frame decisions, i.e. content-dependent (inference, not tested). The point estimates (0.52 and 0.56) are near chance but the rule is not relaxed after the fact.
  - SET S (bit_rate, file_size; content-complexity proxy, not part of the rule): logistic regression 0.548 [0.494, 0.602]; gradient boosting 0.507 [0.447, 0.567]; single-feature 0.560 (generated lower). Leave-one-generator-out (LR / GBT): BDAnimateDiffLightning 0.666 / 0.478, CogVideoX5B 0.514 / 0.521, RunwayML 0.555 / 0.502, StableDiffusion 0.418 / 0.452, VideoPoet 0.557 / 0.493, Veo 0.619 / 0.532. SET H + S combined: LR 0.586 [0.534, 0.637], GBT 0.547 [0.489, 0.604].
  - Pixel/temporal residual baseline on v2 (same 7 statistics, same rule as v1): logistic regression 0.774 [0.730, 0.818], gradient boosting 0.792 [0.748, 0.833]; verdict residual cues REMAIN (lower bound > 0.70). Single-feature: mad_std 0.650, sat_mean 0.627, lapvar_mean 0.626, luma_mean 0.608, lapvar_std 0.580, mad_mean 0.563. Leave-one-generator-out (LR / GBT): BDAnimateDiffLightning 0.506 / 0.511, CogVideoX5B 0.784 / 0.790, RunwayML 0.792 / 0.709, StableDiffusion 0.816 / 0.733, VideoPoet 0.820 / 0.797, Veo 0.702 / 0.736. Essentially the v1 picture (0.76), slightly higher.
  - DF26 (descriptive only, no classifier fitted): 2,691 canonical-v2 clips, all colour tags identical in real and fake (see above); further descriptive statistics by label and generator are in e019_v2_results.json (df26_descriptive_only).
  - Remaining confounds: one real source (100 Pexels clips) against 2,505 generated clips; bit rate and file size and GOP statistics carry content complexity; pixel statistics separate at about 0.78 AUC (camera footage vs synthetic motion, resampling differences), generator dependent; first 2 s, 224 px crop only.
  - Verdict: v2 closes the perfect colour-tag shortcut and all categorical header cues, but the preregistered acceptance rule is NOT passed (GBT upper bound 0.605 against 0.60, caused by the GOP statistics). Strictly by the rule, v2 clips are NOT declared acceptable, and the E-020 gate ("header-metadata shortcut removed") is not met. Practical reading, not a rule outcome: no header field separates the classes (all single-feature AUCs below 0.60, point estimates 0.52 and 0.56), so metadata cannot be a perfect shortcut any more; content-based experiments on v2 clips are defensible only if reported together with the SET S and pixel-statistics baselines (C3 in E-020) and with this failure stated. Whether to proceed is the user's decision.
  - Proposed v3 (NOT applied, needs a decision): add `-x264-params no-info=1:scenecut=0:b-adapt=0` (fixed GOP and B-frame pattern, so GOP statistics become constant by construction) and re-run the same audit with the same acceptance rule; SET S would remain a content proxy. Alternative: declare the GOP statistics content-complexity proxies (move them to SET S) and re-evaluate; that is a post-hoc rule change after seeing this result and would be tagged as such.

- E-024 RESULT, 2026-10-26 (script scripts/lipsync_eval/e024.py; results data/_results/platform/e024_lipsync_validation.json and e024_excluded_e022_clips.json; frozen module syncnet-v2-port-2, nothing modified or retuned; run once; cuda RTX 5070; 0 ffmpeg failures, 0 script errors; weights-licence note unchanged and unresolved). The 40 E-022 clips were excluded (assert passed). N as preregistered: A 60, B 60, C 4 x 40, D 240, E 60, F 100, G 70. Wilson 95 percent intervals in brackets. Decided autonomously - needs user review.
  - L-A (LAV-DF train, fresh 60): potential_mismatch 9/60 = 15.0 percent [8.1, 26.1], consistent 50, inconclusive 1. FAILED (line 10 percent; upper bound above 20 percent). The 9 flags are confident non-zero offsets (-8 to -3 frames, one +4); the unmodified files themselves measure off-sync, as in E-022 (12.5 percent there), so the false-alarm rate on synchronised speech of about 12 to 15 percent is reproduced on new clips.
  - L-B (FakeAVCeleb dev_x RVRA, 60 identities): potential_mismatch 8/60 = 13.3 percent [6.9, 24.2], consistent 50, inconclusive 1, not_applicable 1. FAILED (same line, same pattern; offsets -5 to -3 frames). Both sources agree, so this is not a LAV-DF peculiarity; the 2 frame tolerance sits inside the natural spread of real clips (offsets -3 and below on about 14 percent of real clips).
  - L-D-in (in-range shifts, 180 shifted files): 176/180 = 97.8 percent [94.4, 99.1] with offset error <= 2 frames or flagged: PASSED. Offset error median 0 frames in every shift, 100 percent within 2 frames among completed runs (strict reading 176/176 over the files that returned an offset; 4 runs inconclusive: 1 at -480, 3 at +480, offset at the boundary or low confidence). Flag rate (potential_mismatch) by shift: -480 29/30, -240 28/30, -120 11/30, +120 24/30, +240 29/30, +480 27/30; the rest consistent (-120: 19 consistent, because 3 frames is near the tolerance and clips already carry offsets). L-D-480: false consistent 0/60 [0, 6.0]: PASSED.
  - L-D-out (+-1000 ms, 60 files): 60/60 inconclusive, consistent 0/60 [0, 6.0], potential_mismatch 0/60: PASSED. (Expected after the E-022 changes; this time on clips not used to set them.)
  - L-E (+-40 ms): potential_mismatch 15/60 = 25.0 percent [15.8, 37.2] (-40: 3/30, +40: 12/30): FAILED (line 20 percent, informational). It comes from the same near-tolerance clips as L-A and L-B: a one frame shift pushes a clip already at offset -2 to -3 over the tolerance.
  - L-F (synthetic non-speech, 100 files): 100/100 inconclusive (AUDIO_NOT_SPEECH_LIKE), non-inconclusive 0/100 [0, 3.7]: PASSED. Natural music, singing and speech over noise remain untested (no such audio on disk).
  - L-G (unsuitable, 70 files): no audio 20/20 not_applicable, no face 20/20 not_applicable, under 2 s 20/20 inconclusive, audio-only 10/10 not_applicable: PASSED (0/70 bad [0, 5.2]).
  - (c) Known lip-sync fakes, FakeAVCeleb dev_x, 40 each (no pass or fail assigned): potential_mismatch FVRA/wav2lip 34/40 = 85.0 percent [70.9, 92.9] (consistent 4, inconclusive 2); FVFA/wav2lip 36/40 = 90.0 percent [76.9, 96.0] (consistent 4); FVFA/faceswap-wav2lip 37/40 = 92.5 percent [80.1, 97.4] (consistent 3); FVFA/fsgan-wav2lip 35/40 = 87.5 percent [73.9, 94.5] (consistent 5). THE PREDICTION (a low flag rate on Wav2Lip) WAS WRONG: Wav2Lip clips were flagged at 85 to 93 percent, against 13 to 15 percent on the real clips (L-A, L-B). The measured offsets are almost all exactly -3 frames (FVRA/wav2lip: -3 in 34, -2 in 3, -1 in 1; FVFA/wav2lip -3 in 36, -2 in 4; the other two strata similar), i.e. a systematic offset just beyond the 2 frame tolerance, which is a property of how these clips were generated or muxed (a consistent audio-video lag), not necessarily evidence of manipulation. Reading: the flag separates Wav2Lip clips from real FakeAVCeleb clips on this tolerance, but only through a one frame margin, and it could be a generator or pipeline artefact; it cannot be read as a deployed detector and could be flipped by changing the tolerance from 2 to 3, which would not be allowed after seeing this. A "consistent" verdict on any of these fakes still would not show authenticity. No claim of detection ability is made; no AUC computed.
  - OVERALL: NOT validated. Lines passed: L-D-in, L-D-480, L-D-out, L-F, L-G. Lines failed: L-A (15.0 percent), L-B (13.3 percent), L-E (25.0 percent). The E-022 changes (confidence minimum, speech gate, boundary rule) did generalise to new clips for shifted audio, out-of-range shifts, synthetic non-speech and unsuitable inputs. The 2 frame tolerance does not: about 14 percent of unmodified speech clips from two sources fall beyond it. No retuning was done; any change to the tolerance (for example by examining the offset distribution of real clips: -2 to +1 frames cover most) needs a new preregistered experiment on other clips, and the offset distribution above was seen here and cannot be used to claim a validated value from this data. Limitations as preregistered: both sources VoxCeleb2 derived, speakers may overlap with E-022, small N, one decoder path.

## E-019 v3 addendum: canonical protocol v3 (fixed GOP and B-frame structure) and re-audit   (preregistered 2026-10-26, before any v3 file was produced; decided autonomously - needs user review)
- Change v2 to v3: `-x264-params no-info=1:scenecut=0:b-adapt=0` added to the ffmpeg arguments (single definition: dfd.platform.genvideo.canonical.ffmpeg_args, PROTOCOL_ID canonical-v3). Reason: the only non-constant SET H features in v2 were three GOP statistics (gop_mean_i_dist, gop_n_i_in_60, gop_frac_b), which come from x264 content-adaptive scene-cut and B-frame decisions (inference, not tested); with scenecut and adaptive B-frames off, the GOP structure is fixed by construction.
- ACCEPTANCE RULE, copied verbatim from the v2 addendum and NOT changed: "(a) SET H metadata-only grouped-CV AUC upper 95 percent bound below 0.60 for BOTH models AND (b) every SET H feature is constant across labels or has direction-free single-feature AUC below 0.60. Both needed." SET H and SET S definitions are unchanged. SET S (bit_rate, file_size) and the pixel/temporal residual baseline are reported exactly as in v2 and are not part of the rule. Same audit (grouped 5-fold by prompt id, LR and depth-2 GBT, 2,000-resample group bootstrap, seed 0), same colour-tag ffprobe check on at least 60 clips per group, plus ffprobe -show_frames on 20 clips to confirm identical GOP structure.
- Outputs (git-ignored): data/_canonical_v3/{deepaction,df26}/, data/_results/canonical_v3/. Scripts: scripts/canonical/canonicalise_v3.py, reaudit_v3.py. DF26 descriptive only, no classifier fitted on DF26 labels.
- History caveat (stated plainly): v1 (failed: perfect colour-tag shortcut) and v2 (failed the rule by a small margin: GBT upper bound 0.605) are preserved with their results. v3 is the THIRD protocol tried on the same data, and its design was informed by the audit results of v1 and v2. Choosing a protocol after seeing audit results is a form of researcher degrees of freedom, so any pass is conditional on that history and cannot be read as a blind test. The pixel-residual baseline remains the key control. If v3 fails, no v4 is justified: further tuning of the protocol on these results is not defensible, and the practical option is to report the residual cues.
- Prediction (not a measurement): GOP features constant; SET H AUC about 0.5 with both upper bounds below 0.60; SET S and pixel baseline about as in v2 (0.55 and 0.78).
- Result: (to be appended below after the run)
- E-023 RESULT, 2026-10-26 (development half only; scripts/e023/measure.py and scripts/e023/e023.py; outputs data/_results/e023/: measure.json, fvra_all_probe.csv, prep.csv, scores.csv, e023_results.json, canon/ (not in git); no test_x clip opened; ledger untouched; frozen checkpoint I-5-freeze-candidate via RealAdapter; preregistration above unchanged). Decided autonomously - needs user review. 0 transcode failures and 0 scoring failures in every set. Sets: C1 250 RVRA, C1L 250 RVRA, C2 500 fakes, H 272 h264 FVRA (176 identities, all dev faceswap FVRA), K 272 random mpeg4 FVRA (171 identities, seed 20261026). Bootstrap over identities, 2,000 replicates, seed 20261026, 95 percent interval, shared resampling for every AUC and difference.
  - Deviation to report: ffmpeg mpeg4 average-bit-rate mode overshot the target on these short clips. Achieved median bit rate of the intermediate files: recipe M (target 317,957) 404,229; recipe L (target 180,000) 270,018; fakes through M (C2) 351,121. All intermediates were mpeg4. M and L therefore bracket the measured fake interquartile range (280,840 to 433,385) rather than hitting its median. Not rerun; stated here.
  - VERIFIED (dev_x, N as above). Unmatched canonical baseline B (RVRA vs 500 fakes) 0.8763 [0.8548, 0.8963]; original files 0.9768 [0.9679, 0.9845]. Codec-matched primary P1 (RVRA through mpeg4 M, then canonical) 0.8856 [0.8643, 0.9056]; change P1 - B +0.0092 [+0.0006, +0.0180]. Sensitivity S1 (recipe L) 0.9059 [0.8858, 0.9241]; S1 - B +0.0296 [+0.0196, +0.0397]. By the fixed lines both P1 and S1 have lower bound above 0.70: "manipulation evidence remains" (overall verdict under the preregistered rule). Reverse control R1 (fakes put through mpeg4 M once more, reals canonical only) 0.8334 [0.8085, 0.8559]; R1 - B -0.0430 [-0.0528, -0.0339]. Both sides through M (C1 reals vs C2 fakes) 0.8435 [0.8188, 0.8664]; change -0.0328 [-0.0464, -0.0203]. Median scores: RVRA canonical 0.0166, RVRA after M 0.0153, after L 0.0110; fakes canonical 0.2836, fakes after an extra M 0.2199. Per method P1 against B (change): wav2lip 0.8084 vs 0.7930 (+0.0154 [+0.0011, +0.0296], 261 fakes), fsgan 0.9548 vs 0.9512 (+0.0036), faceswap-wav2lip 0.9807 vs 0.9800, fsgan-wav2lip 0.9737 vs 0.9712, faceswap 0.9958 vs 0.9948 (26 fakes); only wav2lip shows an interval excluding 0.
  - VERIFIED codec split (FVRA only, against RVRA). H (h264, no mpeg4 history, all faceswap): original 0.9825 [0.9685, 0.9940], canonical 0.9792 [0.9646, 0.9911], against C1 reals 0.9794 [0.965, 0.9913]; median score 0.986 original, 0.917 canonical. K (mpeg4, fsgan and wav2lip): original 0.9691 [0.9573, 0.9793], canonical 0.8627 [0.8366, 0.8879], against C1 reals 0.8727 [0.8471, 0.8979]; median score 0.5615 original, 0.2406 canonical. Differences A_H - A_K: original +0.0134 [-0.0040, +0.0296], canonical +0.1165 [+0.0892, +0.1441], against C1 +0.1067 [+0.0798, +0.1354].
  - Prediction check (not measurements): P1 predicted 0.70 to 0.85, observed 0.886 (wrong, higher); R1 within 0.03 of B predicted, observed -0.043 (wrong); A_H canonical above 0.90 predicted, observed 0.979 (right).
  - What this resolves (inference from dev evidence only). Giving the real clips an mpeg4 generation at a rate bracketing the fakes' did not reduce the frozen score's separation (P1 is not below B, it is slightly above), and the scores of real clips did not move (median 0.0166 to 0.0153). So the preregistered "codec shortcut explains the residual" outcome is not supported, and a pure explanation of the residual 0.877 as an extra mpeg4 generation carried by the fakes is not supported by this control. The fakes lose some separation with an extra generation (R1 - B -0.043), so the fakes' score depends partly on how much compression they carry, which is the direction a compression-history sensitivity would predict, but the real class does not respond in the matched direction.
  - What this does NOT resolve. (1) Matching is approximate (stream parameters copied, bit rates bracketed, generator pipeline unknown, real clips pass through h264, mpeg4, h264 while fakes' earlier steps are unknown); a codec-matched real clip is not identical in noise history to a generated fake. (2) The h264 fakes (H) keep 0.979 after canonical, but codec is completely confounded with method in FakeAVCeleb (all h264 FVRA are faceswap, all fsgan and wav2lip are mpeg4), so H vs K differences (canonical +0.12) cannot be assigned to codec rather than method or content; the low wav2lip score (0.79) is equally consistent with a method that edits only the mouth region. (3) Other channel differences (resolution history, identity/content, recording sessions, the real clips being unedited full-frame footage) are untouched by this control. (4) This does not say anything about the sealed half: whether the E-015 sealed 0.955 is inflated, and by how much, is not estimated here and is not claimed. Development evidence suggests the residual after canonical re-encoding is not explained by an added mpeg4 generation alone; it does not establish that it is manipulation evidence rather than another dataset artefact.

This is a statement about the container and stream headers only. The pass is conditional on the history above: v3 is the third protocol tried on the same data and its GOP change was chosen after the v2 audit; it is not a blind test.

SET H + S: LR 0.550 [0.497, 0.604], GBT 0.479 [0.424, 0.535] (identical to S, since H adds nothing). Verdict from the v1 line: INCONCLUSIVE (LR upper bound 0.604), reported, not part of the rule.

Single-feature: mad_std 0.650, sat_mean 0.627, lapvar_mean 0.625, luma_mean 0.608, lapvar_std 0.579, mad_mean 0.563. Essentially unchanged from v1 (0.76) and v2 (0.77 and 0.79): the protocol change did not touch pixel-level cues, as expected.

  - Verdict: the E-020 gate "header-metadata shortcut removed" is met by the preregistered rule on v3 clips, with the conditionality stated. The remaining separability (pixel statistics about 0.78 AUC, bit rate and file size about 0.56) is content and source difference (one real source of 100 Pexels clips against 2,505 generated clips), not a header artefact; any content-based result on v3 clips must be reported next to the pixel-residual baseline (C3 in E-020). No v4 is needed or to be pursued.

E-020 therefore uses canonical protocol v3 (data/_canonical_v3, src/dfd/platform/genvideo/canonical.py PROTOCOL_ID canonical-v3) wherever the text above says canonical-v2; everything else in the E-020 preregistration is unchanged. The v3 pass is conditional on the protocol history (v1, v2, v3 tried on the same data), and the pixel-statistics control C3 stays the key comparator.

Leave-one-generator-out over prompt-id folds, macro AUC over the six generators, 2,000-resample cluster bootstrap over prompt groups:

Heads frozen and hashed before DF26 evaluation: {"C1": "09bfc67f0e1e8f1cf217b69fc35da409dafe162580de955eea2cd7c4d06dcab7", "C2": "c0f4b6d3156976c51f47bb55580a75cbff8197c7c6746ce4fd4d6b6a2406aeb1", "C3": "4af9925ecaf9b213e3d801ff745dd66487fb61ab1091af0dad489231a6997ab2"}.
  - Predictions check: C1 0.65 to 0.85 predicted, 0.507 observed (prediction wrong); C3 0.55 to 0.75 predicted, 0.761 observed (just above); C2 was a control and exceeded both. Caveat to carry into the interpretation: C2 is an appearance classifier, so on DeepAction it may recognise stock-footage content versus generated action content (a source confound, 100 real clips from one library) as much as generation artefacts; the DF26 evaluation, which compares real and generated talking-head videos of the same scenarios, is the test of that. The module built from the chosen head is therefore NOT the ReStraV method: it is an appearance baseline on frozen DINOv2-small, and its documentation must say so.

- E-020 DF26 RESULT, 2026-10-26 (held-out, evaluation only, run once with `scripts/genvideo/e020.py df26 --final`, exit 0; results data/_results/genvideo/e020_df26_results.json; ledger/final_ledger.jsonl entry iteration E-020, dataset df26; head hashes verified equal to the values frozen at the development RESULT above; DF26 features in data/_results/genvideo/features_df26.npz, 0 feature failures, features_df26_failures.json is empty). 2,691 clips (271 real, 2,420 generated; closed 1,065 clips with 794 fakes, open 1,897 clips with 1,626 fakes). Cluster bootstrap over source groups, 2,000 resamples, 95 percent interval. No DF26 number was used to train, tune, select or set a threshold.
  - Primary (preregistered): chosen head C2, AUC on the closed-generator subset 0.4880 [0.4534, 0.5204]. The upper bound is below 0.60, so H-G1 is NOT SUPPORTED by the preregistered rule.
  - C2 (appearance baseline, mean DINOv2-small CLS + logistic regression): all 0.4957 [0.4755, 0.5168]; open 0.4995 [0.4820, 0.5165]; at the fixed development threshold 0.9623: sensitivity 0.3165, specificity 0.6568. Per generator AUC range 0.4118 (HunyuanVideo 1.5 t2v) to 0.5607 (LTX 2.3 i2v); three generators have an interval excluding 0.5 (LTX 2.3 i2v and Wan 2.2 i2v above, HunyuanVideo 1.5 t2v below); none is near 0.7.
  - C1 (21 trajectory values, MLP): all 0.5077 [0.4723, 0.5424]; closed 0.5337 [0.4954, 0.5724]; open 0.4949 [0.4593, 0.5315]. Per generator 0.3922 (HunyuanVideo t2v) to 0.6124 (Wan 2.6).

Per generator 0.3172 (Kling 3.0) to 0.7236 (Veo 3.1): the sign flips between generators, so this is generator-specific pixel-statistic difference, not a stable generation cue.
  - Interpretation (evidence): none of the three heads separates generated from real DF26 clips usefully; the DeepAction LOGO advantage of C2 (0.828) did not transfer. Prediction check: the pre-run prediction was a DF26 closed-subset AUC of 0.50 to 0.65 with H-G1 more likely not supported or inconclusive; observed 0.488 (just below the predicted range, same conclusion).
  - Interpretation (hypotheses, not tested here): C2's DeepAction result probably reflects a source/appearance confound (100 real stock clips from one library against generated action footage) rather than generation artefacts; the DF26 design (real and generated of the same scenario, talking heads) removes that cue. C3's slightly higher DF26 AUC is weak evidence and is explained by per-generator pixel differences (some below 0.5, some above), not by a detector. Limitation: the evaluation is one training corpus against one test corpus, first 2 s only, 224 px centre crop, so it does not show the method cannot work, only that this adaptation trained on DeepAction does not generalise to DF26.
  - Consequence (autonomous, needs user review): the module may be installed only as an explicitly experimental, non-validated capability whose tested_scope quotes the lines above; see D-071 addendum.

- E-020 post-hoc descriptive diagnostic, 2026-10-26 (scripts/genvideo/e020_diagnostic.py, output data/_results/genvideo/e020_diagnostic.json; frozen head_C2, no fitting, nothing selected or changed; written after the DF26 result and not preregistered, so it is exploratory description only). On DeepAction the head scores are IN-SAMPLE (the final head was trained on all 2,605 clips): real median 0.0057, generated median 1.0, i.e. the training set is almost perfectly separated, which says nothing about generalisation (the out-of-fold figure is the LOGO 0.8281). On DF26 the score distributions of real and generated clips are indistinguishable: real quantiles (5, 25, 50, 75, 95 percent) 0.0001, 0.0184, 0.6386, 0.9878, 1.0; generated 0.0001, 0.0179, 0.5478, 0.9872, 1.0; about 26 percent of real and 25 percent of generated clips are saturated (below 0.001 or above 0.999). DF26 mean embeddings lie 0.54 DeepAction standard deviations (per dimension, RMS) from the DeepAction mean. DF26 score vs mean luminance Spearman 0.128; DF26 label vs mean luminance -0.209. Reading: the head is confident on DF26 but its confidence is unrelated to the label, consistent with it having learned DeepAction-specific appearance; this does not identify which cue.

## E-025: empirical offset band for the lip-sync module, calibrated on one pool and validated on a disjoint pool   (preregistered 2026-10-26, before any E-025 clip was run; decided autonomously - needs user review)
- Status: PLANNED. Motivation: E-022 and E-024 showed that the placeholder tolerance of +-2 frames (syncnet-v2-port-2) flags about 14 percent of unmodified speech clips (E-024 L-A 15.0 percent [8.1, 26.1], L-B 13.3 percent [6.9, 24.2]), and that Wav2Lip clips are flagged 85 to 93 percent almost only through an offset of exactly -3 frames. What I knew before writing this: the E-022 and E-024 offset patterns above (real clips at -3 or below on about 14 percent). The band below is therefore NOT a blind choice of design; the rule and its numbers are fixed here before any new clip is scored, and the calibration and validation pools are disjoint from each other and from every E-022 and E-024 clip.
- Question: does a band of offsets fitted to the natural spread of real speech clips (stage 1) keep the false-alarm rate on real clips low on independent real clips (stage 2), and what does it do to the flag rate of Wav2Lip-based fakes? A pass is a statement about VoxCeleb2-derived talking-head clips only.
- Data (development roles only; nothing sealed; FakeAVCeleb test_x is never opened; licence terms of FakeAVCeleb stay unresolved and no clip is redistributed): LAV-DF train clips with no video or audio modification, 3 s or longer (the e024.lavdf_pool rule); FakeAVCeleb dev_x RealVideo-RealAudio clips, one clip per identity group. Exclusions: the 40 E-022 clips, the 60 E-024 LAV-DF clips, the 60 E-024 FakeAVCeleb real clips and their groups, the 160 E-024 FakeAVCeleb fake clips. Seed 20261028. Pools: CAL-L 150 and VAL-L 150 LAV-DF clips (consecutive blocks of one seeded shuffle); CAL-F 80 and VAL-F 80 FakeAVCeleb real clips with distinct identity groups across and within the two pools; W 4 x 40 FakeAVCeleb dev_x fakes (FVRA wav2lip, FVFA wav2lip, FVFA faceswap-wav2lip, FVFA fsgan-wav2lip; pick_by_identity, excluding E-024 C clips), stage 2 only.
- Procedure: the frozen module syncnet-v2-port-2 is used as a measuring instrument without any change (MIN_CONFIDENCE 3.0 and speech gate unchanged). Stage 1: run it on CAL-L and CAL-F; keep clips whose outcome is consistent or potential_mismatch (a confident offset). Band rule (fixed now): LO is the 2.5th and HI the 97.5th percentile of the pooled confident offsets (method "lower" for LO and "higher" for HI on integer offsets), so that at least 95 percent of confident calibration clips lie inside [LO, HI]. If HI - LO exceeds 10 frames (400 ms) the experiment stops there: offset cannot be used and the module stays unchanged. Stage 2: the band is frozen and the offsets of VAL-L, VAL-F and W recorded by the same unchanged module; "flagged" means a confident clip (confidence rule unchanged) whose offset lies outside [LO, HI].
- Lines (fixed now): V1 false alarms on VAL (VAL-L plus VAL-F, denominator all 230 clips, Wilson 95 percent interval): PASS if the point rate is 10 percent or lower and the upper bound is below 20 percent; if the point rate is 10 percent or lower but the upper bound is 20 percent or more it is WEAK. V2 per source (VAL-L, VAL-F) point rate 10 percent or lower for each, reported, same rule. V3 (informational, no pass line) flag rate of each W stratum under the band and, for comparison, under the old +-2 band; and, on 30 VAL-L clips with the audio delayed by +-480 ms, +-240 ms and +-120 ms (E-024 recipe, e024-style expected offset), the fraction flagged under the band. Decision rule: only if V1 is PASS and V2 holds is a new module version (syncnet-v2-port-3, asymmetric band [LO, HI] as a constructor argument, default the old +-2 behaviour unchanged for tests) allowed, and its documentation must say the band was fitted to VoxCeleb2-derived clips and that it removes the Wav2Lip offset cue if V3 shows so. Otherwise the module stays at port-2 and the outcome is documented.
- Predictions (not measurements): pooled calibration 95 percent band between [-5, +2] frames; V1 point rate 3 to 8 percent; W flag rates under the band fall below 50 percent in every stratum (the -3 offset lies inside the band); +-120 ms shifts (3 frames) are flagged in under half of the cases.
- Known limitations in advance: both sources derive from VoxCeleb2, so the band says nothing about other media; the natural offset may come from the data pipeline (a constant lag) and not from speakers; the confident-only calibration excludes clips on which SyncNet had no clear minimum, which are reported as the inconclusive fraction; the module does not detect manipulation either way, and Wav2Lip flag rates are an informational property of the offset cue, not a detection claim.
- Result: (to be appended below after the run)
- E-025 amendment, 2026-10-26 (appended before any E-025 clip was scored; no result seen): the preregistered exclusion of "the 160 E-024 FakeAVCeleb fake clips and their groups" leaves only 96 of the 250 dev_x identity groups, too few for two pools of 80 distinct groups. Corrected rule: the FakeAVCeleb real pools (CAL-F, VAL-F) exclude the 60 E-024 real clips and their identity groups only; the Wav2Lip fakes (W) exclude the 160 E-024 fake clips (clip level). Identities of the E-024 fakes may therefore also appear among the E-025 real clips (different clips). All other text of the preregistration is unchanged. Script: scripts/lipsync_eval/e025.py.

- Overnight review note, 2026-10-26 (E-019, E-021, E-023; no new measurement). (1) E-019: the v1, v2 and v3 blocks, their scripts and result files (data/_results/canonical{,_v2,_v3}/) are all present and none was rewritten. The v3 acceptance rule (SET H features constant or direction-free AUC below 0.60) is satisfied trivially by construction, since every SET H feature is constant, so the pass establishes only that container, stream and GOP header fields carry no label information in v3 clips; it does not establish absence of pixel-level, bit-rate or content shortcuts (pixel baseline 0.773 to 0.787, bit rate and file size 0.55), and the phrase "shortcut-free" is not supported. (2) E-021 and E-023: every decimal number in the E-023 RESULT block was matched against data/_results/e023/e023_results.json and measure.json by script (the only non-matching values were the preregistered constants 0.70 and a quoted earlier residual 0.877); E-021 was spot-checked the same way (non-matching values come from neighbouring text, not from its result lines). The results separate as follows. Measured: a codec-matched real class did not lower the frozen visual score's separation (P1 0.8856 vs B 0.8763), fakes lose some separation after an extra generation (R1 - B -0.0430), h264 fakes keep 0.979 after canonical re-encode. Plausible, untested: the residual 0.877 reflects a method or content difference (Wav2Lip edits only the mouth region). Unresolved: whether the sealed E-015 0.955 is inflated by a dataset artefact; the sealed half stays untouched. (3) Smallest further analysis without touching the sealed half: none justified; the codec-method confound inside FakeAVCeleb (h264 only with faceswap, mpeg4 only with fsgan and wav2lip) cannot be separated by any development-half analysis.

- E-020 reproducibility check, 2026-10-26 (scripts/genvideo/repro_check.py, output data/_results/genvideo/repro_check.json; an integrity check, no accuracy content). 8 DeepAction and 8 DF26 clips (seed 20261026) were canonicalised from the ORIGINAL files by the module's own function: 16 of 16 decoded frame arrays are bit-identical to the stored canonical-v3 clips used in the experiment, the live mean-CLS feature path reproduces the stored feature table, and the head_C2 score differs from the stored-feature score by at most 0.0 (6 decimals); the installed head.npz gives exactly the frozen head's scores (same weights, new metadata only). So the live module computes what E-020 evaluated; the live path is reproducible on this machine (same ffmpeg, GPU and library versions; no cross-machine claim).

- E-025 RESULT, 2026-10-26 (script scripts/lipsync_eval/e025.py, run once; outputs data/_results/platform/e025_band.json (stage 1, written before stage 2) and e025_validation.json; log data/_logs/e025.log, exit 0, 0 ffmpeg failures; frozen module syncnet-v2-port-2 used unchanged as the measuring instrument; cuda; preregistration and amendment above unchanged; Wilson 95 percent intervals). Decided autonomously - needs user review.
  - Stage 1 (CAL, 150 LAV-DF + 80 FakeAVCeleb real, 224 confident, 6 inconclusive or not applicable): offset histogram -9:1, -7:1, -6:6, -5:4, -4:11, -3:21, -2:47, -1:57, 0:43, 1:27, 2:4, 3:1, 4:1; median -1. Band by the fixed rule: [-6, +2] frames (width 8, stop rule not triggered), 98.2 percent of confident calibration clips inside.
  - Stage 2 (VAL, 150 LAV-DF + 80 FakeAVCeleb real, disjoint): V1 flagged 3 of 230 = 1.3 percent [0.4, 3.8] under the band: PASS (the same clips under the old +-2 rule: 27 of 230 = 11.7 percent). V2 LAV-DF 3 of 150 = 2.0 percent [0.7, 5.7], FakeAVCeleb 0 of 80 [0, 4.6]: both hold. new_version_allowed = true by the preregistered rule.
  - V3 (informational): Wav2Lip-based fakes (4 strata x 40, FakeAVCeleb dev_x): flagged under the band 0 of 40 in every stratum; under the old +-2 rule 37/39, 36/40, 37/39, 34/39 confident clips. All confident offsets are -3 (34 to 37 per stratum) or -2 (2 to 5). Audio shifted by the E-022 sign convention (30 LAV-DF clips, 28 with a confident base offset), flagged under the band: -480 ms 26/28, -240 ms 26/28, -120 ms 14/28, +120 ms 2/28, +240 ms 14/28, +480 ms 26/28 (old rule: 26, 26, 14, 20, 28, 26).
  - Prediction check (not measurements): band predicted within [-5, +2], observed [-6, +2] (slightly wider); V1 predicted 3 to 8 percent, observed 1.3 percent (lower); Wav2Lip flag rates below 50 percent: right (0); +-120 ms shifts flagged in under half: -120 ms 14/28 is exactly half (not under), +120 ms 2/28 (right).
  - Reading. Measured: real speech clips from these two VoxCeleb2-derived sources have a broad confident offset distribution centred near -1 frame, and a band covering 95 percent of it keeps false alarms on disjoint real clips near 1 percent. Consequence, also measured: the earlier Wav2Lip flag rate (85 to 93 percent, E-024) disappears completely, because Wav2Lip clips lie at -3 and -2 frames, inside the real clips' natural spread; the offset cue therefore does not separate these fakes from real clips once the false-alarm rate is controlled, and the E-024 separation was an artefact of the narrow +-2 rule. Sensitivity to genuine audio shifts also falls in the negative direction (a 120 ms shift is flagged in half of the clips). Not established: why the real spread is this wide (SyncNet's own bias, the VoxCeleb2 pipeline or speakers) and whether the band transfers to any other media; both sources derive from VoxCeleb2.
  - Action under the preregistered decision rule: module version syncnet-v2-port-3 with the asymmetric band (-6, +2) as the default, the old symmetric tolerance still available (offset_band=None); module documentation updated with the numbers above. The module remains weak evidence; no claim of lip-sync fake detection is made, and a consistent result still does not show authenticity.
  - Post-audit clarification, 2026-10-26 (no new measurement; no number, band or conclusion above was changed). Observed facts: the 230 calibration and 230 validation clips are disjoint by file path (LAV-DF) and by sample id and identity group (FakeAVCeleb); the figures 3/230, 1.3 percent [0.4, 3.8], 27/230 = 11.7 percent, 0/160 and the shift counts were recomputed from data/_results/platform/e025_validation.json and match. Limitation that narrows the claim: the LAV-DF half (150 + 150 clips) was split by file, not by speaker. The LAV-DF metadata (file, n_fakes, fake_periods, duration, original, modify_video, modify_audio, split, frame counts) has no speaker or source-identity field, so the same speaker can occur in both pools and speaker-level overlap cannot be excluded or measured. Only the FakeAVCeleb half (80 + 80) was separated by identity group. The false-alarm improvement is therefore numerically reproducible for the evaluated clips but is NOT evidence of speaker-independent generalisation; the Wilson intervals treat clips as independent and may be optimistic if clips of one speaker are correlated. Wording: the sentence "the E-024 separation was an artefact of the narrow rule" is an interpretation. What was measured: Wav2Lip clips sit at offset -3 or -2 (all 157 clips with a confident offset: 34 to 37 per stratum at -3, 2 to 5 at -2), and offset -3 also occurs in 21 of 224 (9.4 percent) real calibration clips, so a cue that is present in most Wav2Lip clips and in about one real clip in ten was removed together with the false alarms. Not established: whether the Wav2Lip offset is a property of those generators, of the FakeAVCeleb pipeline or of SyncNet. The module remains weak evidence and is not a lip-sync deepfake detector; the 0 of 160 Wav2Lip result and the partial 120 ms shift sensitivity (2 to 14 of 28) stay in its tested scope and limitations.

## E-026: source-confound test of the installed experimental generated-video head on real videos from other sources (roadmap X2, with the X1 reproduction as its gate)   (preregistered 2026-10-27, before any E-026 clip was canonicalised or scored; decided autonomously within the user's instruction of 2026-10-27 - needs user review)
- Status: COMPLETED 2026-10-27 (result appended below; this line said PLANNED when the entry was registered, nothing else above the result was changed). Ordering record: this entry is not committed (the user has not authorised commits on 2026-10-27), so git cannot show that it precedes the run; its SHA-256 and the time are written to data/_results/genvideo/e026/prereg.json by the script before the first clip is read (weaker evidence than a commit; docs/RESEARCH_INTEGRITY.md section 2).
- Question: does the installed head (checkpoints/genvideo-E020/head.npz, head_id E020-C2-seed0, SHA-256 b67d01ac0001ec69f53ce659c88ab8b4415bc3fb810ce30041651de3bfa7fafb; logistic regression on the mean DINOv2-small CLS embedding of 8 canonical-v3 frames, experimental, gate closed) score unmodified real videos from sources other than DeepAction's Pexels stock clips as "generated-like"? E-020 interpreted C2's DeepAction result as probably confounded by source and content (all 100 real clips from one library); this was never tested.
- Gate (roadmap X1): recompute C2's out-of-fold DeepAction scores from the stored features (data/_results/genvideo/features_deepaction.npz) with E-020's own fit, fold and LOGO code (scripts/genvideo/e020.py, imported, unchanged). PASS if the macro LOGO AUC equals the recorded 0.8281 to 4 decimals. On FAIL: stop, record, score nothing.
- Data (development roles only; nothing sealed, DF26 not read): (R-FF) all 140 FaceForensics++ original (real) videos of the project's val split, selected with dfd.data.loaders.select_records(final=False), which refuses test splits; (R-LAV) 300 LAV-DF real clips (metadata n_fakes = 0, modify_video and modify_audio false) from the official train directory, which the project split assigns to train (the project's sealed LAV-DF part is the official test directory), drawn with numpy default_rng(20261027) from the 21,254 eligible, sorted by file name before sampling. LAV-DF clips are 224 x 224 face crops of VoxCeleb2-derived talking heads; FF++ originals are YouTube videos at c23. Reference (R-DA): the 100 DeepAction real clips, scored out of fold (mean of each clip's six LOGO out-of-fold scores, as in E-020). The installed head's in-sample scores of the DeepAction reals are reported separately, labelled in-sample.
- Procedure: each R-FF and R-LAV clip goes through the same live path as the module (genvideo.canonical.canonicalise, read_frames, features.PICK, DinoRunner cls_mean, Head.score) with the frozen head; nothing is fitted, selected or calibrated; no head, threshold, checkpoint or ledger entry is written. Outputs only in data/_results/genvideo/e026/ (new folder): prereg.json, x1_reproduction.json, scores.csv (one row per clip: source, sample id, path, score or failure), e026_results.json.
- Threshold: the installed head's own threshold (0.9623, chosen on DeepAction out-of-fold scores in E-020), read from the head file and checked against e020_dev_results.json; it is used only to count flags and is not changed.
- Metrics: per source, the share of real clips at or above the threshold (false-positive rate) with a Wilson 95 percent interval; score quantiles (5, 25, 50, 75, 95); AUC of "other real" against R-DA out-of-fold scores (Mann-Whitney; 0.5 = same distribution; higher = other reals look more generated-like), with a 2,000-resample bootstrap over clips (seed 20261027).
- Lines (fixed now): H-X2 (source confound) SUPPORTED if the pooled R-FF plus R-LAV false-positive rate has a Wilson lower bound above both the R-DA out-of-fold false-positive rate (point) and 0.5. NOT SUPPORTED if the pooled Wilson upper bound is at or below the R-DA out-of-fold rate. Otherwise INCONCLUSIVE. Per-source results are reported whatever the pooled line says.
- Predictions (not measurements): R-DA out-of-fold false-positive rate 15 to 35 percent; R-FF and R-LAV rates far higher (above 60 percent), with LAV-DF the highest; so H-X2 more likely supported than not. The demo run of 2026-10-26 (one LAV-DF clip scored 1.0) motivates the prediction but is a quick look, not a measurement.
- Stop and exclusion rules: a clip whose canonical re-encode or 14-frame read fails is listed with its error and excluded; if more than 10 percent of a source fails, that source is reported as failed and not interpreted. DF26 and every sealed split are never opened.
- Known limitations in advance: a high rate shows that the head does not transfer to real footage of other kinds; it does not identify which factor (source, content, resolution, face crops, compression) causes it, because the other sources differ from Pexels in all of them at once. R-LAV may contain several clips of the same speaker (no speaker field), so its interval is optimistic. The out-of-fold reference comes from fold models, the other sources from the installed full-data head. Nothing here changes the module's status: the gate stays closed (APPROVED_HEADS empty) whatever the result.
- Result: (to be appended below after the run)
- E-026 RESULT, 2026-10-27 (script scripts/genvideo/e026.py, run once at 00:02 to 00:03 +0530; outputs data/_results/genvideo/e026/ (git-ignored; SHA-256: e026_results.json 40ec2f44...f43c, scores.csv 65b7b312...072c, x1_reproduction.json 1b182773...68f6, prereg.json 7dd34fb7...8381); log data/_logs/e026.log; environment Python 3.13.15, Windows 10 19045, RTX 5070 (cuda), DINOv2-small revision ed25f3a3...0056, installed head SHA-256 checked equal to the preregistered value, head threshold 0.9623 checked equal to e020_dev_results.json; scoring 52.9 s for 440 clips).
  - Gate X1: C2 macro LOGO AUC recomputed from the stored features with E-020's own code: 0.8281, recorded 0.8281: PASS.
  - Exclusions: none (0 of 140 R-FF and 0 of 300 R-LAV clips failed the canonical re-encode or the frame read). LAV-DF: 300 drawn from 21,254 eligible official-train real clips.
  - False-positive rate at the head's threshold (share of real clips scored as generated-like), Wilson 95 percent interval, median score: R-DA DeepAction real, out of fold: 19 of 100 = 0.19 [0.1251, 0.2778], median 0.3045. R-FF FaceForensics++ val originals: 72 of 140 = 0.5143 [0.4322, 0.5956], median 0.9811. R-LAV LAV-DF train real: 266 of 300 = 0.8867 [0.8458, 0.9178], median 0.9999. Pooled R-FF + R-LAV: 338 of 440 = 0.7682 [0.7265, 0.8052], median 0.9997. In-sample, for information only (the installed head was trained on these clips): R-DA 0 of 100 = 0.0 [0.0, 0.037], median 0.0057.
  - AUC of other real clips against R-DA out-of-fold scores (0.5 = indistinguishable; higher = other real clips look more generated-like to the head), 2,000 bootstrap resamples over clips: R-FF 0.712 [0.6509, 0.7747]; R-LAV 0.923 [0.8873, 0.9525]; pooled 0.8559 [0.816, 0.8924].
  - H-X2 under the preregistered line: SUPPORTED (pooled lower bound 0.7265 is above the reference rate 0.19 and above 0.5).
  - Prediction check (predictions were not measurements): R-DA out-of-fold rate predicted 15 to 35 percent, observed 19 percent (inside); R-FF and R-LAV predicted above 60 percent with LAV-DF highest: LAV-DF 88.7 percent (as predicted), FF++ 51.4 percent (below the predicted range).
  - Reading. Measured: on unmodified real videos from two sources other than DeepAction's stock library, the installed head scores most clips as more generated-like than its threshold (77 percent pooled; 89 percent of LAV-DF talking-head crops, 51 percent of FF++ YouTube originals), against 19 percent of DeepAction's own real clips out of fold. Interpretation, not established by this design: what the head separates on DeepAction is largely "this library's stock footage" against "everything else", so its DeepAction LOGO AUC of 0.8281 says little about generation; this agrees with its chance-level DF26 result (E-020). Which factor drives it (source, content, face-crop framing, resolution, compression) is not identified, because the other sources differ from Pexels in all of them at once; R-LAV may repeat speakers, so its interval is optimistic. The in-sample rate of 0 shows the installed head fits its 100 training reals almost perfectly, which is why the out-of-fold reference was used.
  - Consequence: no change to the module (the gate stays closed, `APPROVED_HEADS` empty, finding always inconclusive). The tested-scope wording "real videos also receive high scores" can now cite E-026. A DeepAction-only training route is deprioritised; any further generated-video candidate needs matched real and generated sources (X3 under D12, pending the user's decision) and a source-confound control like this one.

## E-027: generated-video heads trained on DF26's open generators, leave-one-family-out and leave-one-generator-out, temporal against frame-pooled features, one counted look at the closed subset (roadmap X3, with X4 and X6)   (preregistered 2026-10-27, before any E-027 feature was extracted or any head was fitted; decided autonomously within D-079 and D-081 - needs user review)
- Status: COMPLETED 2026-10-27 (result appended below; nothing else above the result was changed). Registered wording of this sentence, kept so the preregistration hash can be re-checked by putting it back: "Status: PLANNED (this line is changed to COMPLETED when the result is appended; nothing else above the result will change)." Ordering record: commits are not authorised, so this block's SHA-256 (from the heading line to the line "- Result: (to be appended below after the run)", inclusive) and the time are written to data/_results/modern/e027/prereg.json by scripts/modern_video/e027_prereg.py before the first feature is extracted (weaker evidence than a commit; docs/RESEARCH_INTEGRITY.md section 2).
- Question: can a frozen-encoder head trained on matched real and generated talking-head clips of DF26's six open generators separate real from generated clips of a generator (and of a generator family) it has not seen? Does it use temporal information, or only pooled per-frame appearance? Does in-domain training remove the source confound that E-026 measured for the installed E-020 head?
- Data and licences: DF26 (DATA_LOG G2, LICENSE.md v1.0, D-079). Development pool: the 271 real clips and the 1,626 clips of the six open generators (HunyuanVideo_1.5_14b_i2v and _t2v, LTX_2.3_distilled_i2v and _t2v, Wan_2.2_14b_i2v and _t2v; metadata/generators.csv access = open; 271 each). Closed subset (Grok_Imagine 199, Kling_3.0 198, Veo_3.1 200, Wan_2.6 197; 794 clips): read only by the final step `scripts/modern_video/e027_closed.py --final`, once, for the single candidate frozen before it; never used for training, calibration, threshold, augmentation or selection. The closed subset was already evaluated once by E-020 (C2 0.4880 [0.4534, 0.5204]), so this is the second counted look and is informed by the first. Input clips: the canonical-v3 copies made in E-019 v3 (data/_canonical_v3/df26; first 2.0 s, 7 fps, 14 frames, 224 x 224 centre crop of the short-side-224 resize, fixed codec settings, header shortcut removed by construction), i.e. the pixels path of the live module. The E-020 DINOv2-small features (data/_results/genvideo/features_df26.npz, read only) are reused for IMG-07; its closed rows are masked out at load time in every development step. Encoders, all local and hash-verified in data/_logs/encoder_weights.json (no download): IMG-07 DINOv2-small (Apache-2.0), IMG-08 DINOv2-base (Apache-2.0), IMG-13 CLIP ViT-L/14 OpenAI (CONDITIONAL licence wording, docs/O007_SWEEP.md section 3.1, research use), IMG-17 SigLIP base patch16-224 (Apache-2.0). Feature per frame: CLS after the final norm (DINOv2), projected image embedding (CLIP), attention-pooling output (SigLIP), as in src/dfd/sweep/encoders.py; IMG-07 keeps the stored fp32 E-020 features, the others run in fp16 autocast.
- Source groups and folds (no real clip, and no real clip's paired generated clip, on both sides of a split): group of a real clip = `tc:` plus the first three characters when its clip_id matches `^[0-9]{3}(_[0-9]+)?$` (TalkingCelebs speaker index, for example 000, 000_1 to 000_7), otherwise `yt:` plus the first 11 characters of the clip_id (the YouTube video id of the OpenVid and MAVOS-DD clips; for example XAzplqGuNCc_2_0to257 and XAzplqGuNCc_2_0to257_1 share one group). Every generated clip takes the group of its paired_real_path. The groups are partitioned into 5 folds by a seeded shuffle (numpy default_rng(seed), round robin). Three fold partitions, seeds 0, 1 and 2, are the three seeds of this experiment (the logistic heads are deterministic, so the partition is the main stochastic choice; the MLP of TR21 also uses the seed). Groups are source videos, not verified person identities: the same person in two different source videos would fall into two groups (no identity field exists; limitation).
- Protocols. Primary: leave-one-FAMILY-out (LOFO) over the three open families (HunyuanVideo, LTX, Wan 2.2). Reason: the I2V and T2V variants of one family share the backbone and the VAE decoder, so holding out one variant while training on its sibling measures within-family transfer, not transfer to an unseen generator. Secondary: leave-one-GENERATOR-out (LOGO, six held-out units), the protocol named in the dataset card and licence; reported in full, used for no choice. For held-out unit h and fold f: train on the generated clips of the open generators outside h whose group is not in fold f plus the real clips not in fold f; score the generated clips of h in fold f and the real clips in fold f. Each real clip thus gets one out-of-fold score per held-out unit and seed, each generated clip one per seed. Licence reading (autonomous, needs user review): every training run uses open generators only and every evaluated generator is absent from the run that scores it, so LOFO is the LOGOS rule applied with the family as the held-out unit.
- Metric: AUC of generator g = AUC of the generated clips of g against all real clips, both scored by the runs that hold out g's unit; macro AUC = mean over the six open generators; seed-averaged (mean over fold seeds 0, 1, 2 of the per-seed metric). Intervals: cluster bootstrap over source groups (src/dfd/eval/bootstrap.py, 2,000 resamples, 95 percent percentile, seed 20261027), paired (same resamples) for every difference; the seed standard deviation is reported.
- Candidates. Heads: standardise on the training fold, then logistic regression (C = 1.0, class_weight balanced, lbfgs, max_iter 5000; E-020's C2 head settings, so the training data and encoder are the only change). Features over the canonical frames of one clip, with e_t the frame embedding: P8 = mean of e_t over the 8 frames features.PICK (0 2 4 6 7 9 11 13; the live module's frames); P14 = mean over all 14 frames; PT14 = [mean of e_t, standard deviation of e_t over t, mean over t of |e_(t+1) - e_t|] over the 14 consecutive frames (143 ms apart); PT14s = PT14 with the 14 frames put in a fixed random order per clip before differencing (numpy default_rng(20261027 + clip index)), the frame-order control; D14 = [mean over t of |e_(t+1) - e_t|, standard deviation over t of (e_(t+1) - e_t)], temporal change only, no appearance mean. Selectable candidates (8): P8 and PT14 for each of IMG-07, IMG-08, IMG-13, IMG-17. Diagnostics (not selectable): P14, PT14s and D14 for each encoder; TR21 = E-020's C1 (21 trajectory values from IMG-07, standardised MLP (64, 32), alpha 1e-3, max_iter 500, reals replicated to class balance, random_state = seed). Controls: C3 = the 7 pixel statistics of E-020 (stored) with the same logistic head and protocol; E020 = the installed head checkpoints/genvideo-E020/head.npz (SHA-256 b67d01ac...fafb), scored on the same clips, NOT refitted.
- Hypotheses and lines (fixed now). H-X3 SUPPORTED if at least one selectable candidate has seed-averaged macro LOFO AUC lower bound above 0.60 AND a paired difference to C3 with lower bound above 0; otherwise NOT SUPPORTED (roadmap X3 success line; an upper bound at or below 0.60 for every candidate is reported as the clear negative case). Temporal question H-T (on the encoder of the chosen candidate, and reported for all four): "uses temporal change" if PT14 - P14 has lower bound above 0; "uses frame order" if in addition PT14 - PT14s has lower bound above 0; "no evidence that temporal features add to pooled appearance" if the PT14 - P14 interval contains 0 or lies below it. D14 against 0.5 and against C3, and TR21, are reported as descriptions of what change-only features reach.
- Selection rule (fixed now). Eligible = the H-X3 condition for that candidate. Take the eligible candidate with the highest point estimate; then the simplicity rule of CLAUDE.md rule 4, made precise: if a cheaper eligible candidate has a paired difference to the best whose interval contains 0, choose the cheapest such candidate. Cost order (vision parameters, then features): IMG-07 (22 M) < IMG-08 (87 M) < IMG-17 (93 M) < IMG-13 (304 M), and within an encoder P8 < PT14. If no candidate is eligible, the candidate with the highest macro LOFO point estimate is still frozen and evaluated once on the closed subset, labelled "not eligible", so that the closed result is reported whatever happens.
- Threshold and calibration (X6), from open-subset out-of-fold scores only (fold seed 0, LOFO): t_BA = the score threshold of maximum balanced accuracy on the pooled scores (each generator's clips against the real clips scored for its family; E-020's rule); t_10 = the 90th percentile of the real clips' pooled out-of-fold scores (target 10 percent false-positive rate), secondary. Calibration: Platt scaling (logistic regression on the logit of the score, clipped to [1e-6, 1 - 1e-6]), assessed by leave-one-family-out over the out-of-fold scores (fit on two families' clips and their reals' scores, ECE on the third; 15 bins, src/dfd/eval/metrics.ece), ECE before and after with cluster bootstrap intervals; the final calibrator is fitted on all out-of-fold scores and applied unchanged to the closed subset. Calibrated scores are never used to choose anything.
- Frozen before the closed look (checkpoints/modern-E027/, new folder; meta with encoder, feature, protocol, data, seed, thresholds, calibrator, SHA-256 of the feature table): (a) five cross-fitted fold models of the chosen candidate, fold partition seed 0, each trained on all six open generators' clips outside fold f and the real clips outside fold f; (b) the same five for C3; (c) one deployable model of the chosen candidate trained on all 1,626 open generated clips and all 271 real clips (for the product decision and the source-confound check, NOT for the closed AUC, because it has seen every DF26 real clip). freeze.json lists every hash; the closed script refuses to run if any hash differs.
- Closed look (once; refuses without --final and if data/_results/modern/e027/closed_look.json exists; no ledger write because ledger/** is protected, the marker file stands in). Each closed generated clip is scored by the fold model of its paired real's fold, each real clip by its own fold model, so no scored clip and no clip sharing its source group was in the model's training data. Primary: AUC of the 794 closed clips against the 200 real clips of the two scenarios the commercial generators were run on (Direct_to_Camera_Casual, Studio_Interview), cluster bootstrap over source groups. H-X3c lines (E-020's H-G1 lines, for comparison): SUPPORTED if the lower bound exceeds 0.70, NOT SUPPORTED if the upper bound is below 0.60, otherwise INCONCLUSIVE. Secondary, all reported: AUC against all 271 real clips; AUC and EER per closed generator; at t_BA and at t_10: false-positive rate on the real clips and false-negative rate per closed generator with Wilson 95 percent intervals, confusion matrix, precision, recall and F1 (precision depends on the 794 to 200 class ratio, stated with it), PR-AUC (average precision); ECE of the calibrated scores; paired AUC differences to C3 (cross-fitted, same folds) and to the E020 head, cluster bootstrap. Wan_2.6 shares the "Wan" family label with the open Wan 2.2 training generators (metadata/generators.csv), so its result is reported separately as a same-developer generator, and the other three as different-family generators.
- Source-confound re-check (E-026 clips, development roles only; DF26 not involved): the deployable model scores the 140 FF++ val originals and the 300 LAV-DF official-train real clips selected exactly as in E-026 (scripts/genvideo/e026.py clips_ffpp, which goes through the split guard, and clips_lavdf), through the live path (canonicalise, read_frames, the chosen encoder and feature). False-positive rate at t_BA per source and pooled with Wilson intervals. Lines: CONFOUND PERSISTS if the pooled Wilson lower bound is above both the DF26 real clips' out-of-fold false-positive rate at t_BA (point) and 0.5; NO EXCESS if the pooled upper bound is at or below that rate; otherwise PARTIAL. It runs after the freeze and changes nothing.
- X4 (secondary, open subset only, after the selection; never changes the closed-look candidate): the chosen candidate's encoder on three alternative views of the original DF26 files, each re-encoded with the canonical-v3 codec settings (same libx264 parameters and colour tags; only the filter graph and window differ): C16 = canonical geometry (short side 224, centre crop) with 16 frames spread uniformly over the whole clip; LB16 = whole frame letterboxed into 224 x 224 (long side 224, black padding), 16 frames over the whole clip; NC16 = native-resolution 224 x 224 centre crop (no resize), 16 frames over the whole clip. Features P16 and PT16 (as P14 and PT14 over 16 frames). Comparison: paired differences to the chosen canonical-v3 candidate under LOFO. Success: a positive paired interval; failure: none. The X4 clips are written under data/_results/modern/e027/x4_clips/ (git-ignored, DF26-derived, never shared).
- Error analysis (open out-of-fold, fold seed 0, t_BA; and the closed look): false-negative rate per generator by scenario, speaking_style and camera_type; false-positive rate of real clips by scenario, speaking_style, camera_type and source kind (TalkingCelebs or YouTube group); counts and Wilson intervals; no DF26 frame or clip is saved into any document.
- Predictions (not measurements): best selectable macro LOFO AUC 0.70 to 0.90; LOGO 0.02 to 0.10 above LOFO; C3 0.55 to 0.70; CLIP or SigLIP above DINOv2-small; PT14 - P14 interval containing 0 (no evidence of temporal use at 7 fps over 2 s); closed AUC of the chosen candidate 0.55 to 0.75 (lower than open LOFO), with Wan 2.6 the best closed generator; source-confound false-positive rate on FF++ and LAV-DF well above the DF26 real rate (training on DF26 talking heads alone does not cover other real sources).
- Exclusions and stop rules: a clip whose features cannot be computed is listed and excluded; if more than 2 percent of a generator's clips fail, that generator is reported as failed. A run that fails is logged in the RESULT and its outputs kept.
- Outputs (new folders only): scripts/modern_video/ (e027_prereg.py, e027_features.py, e027_dev.py, e027_x4.py, e027_confound.py, e027_closed.py, e027_lib.py), data/_results/modern/e027/ (prereg.json, groups_folds.csv, features_*.npz, oof_scores.csv, dev_results.json, freeze.json, confound_results.json, x4_results.json, closed_look.json, closed_scores.csv, closed_results.json, error_analysis.json, logs/), checkpoints/modern-E027/. Nothing is installed into the product; APPROVED_HEADS and every threshold stay unchanged.
- Known limitations in advance: 271 real clips in about 220 source groups (small real class; intervals are wide); three open families only, so LOFO has three held-out units; groups are source videos, not person identities; 2.0 s, 7 fps, 224 px canonical clips discard high-frequency detail (X4 checks part of this); DF26 clips are 5 s 720p talking heads and say nothing about other content; the closed subset was looked at once before; a fold model is trained on about four fifths of the data, so the closed result describes the recipe, not the deployable full model exactly.

(2) e027_closed.py: a feature failure on a closed clip raised an assertion after the marker had been written, which would have burnt the counted look; changed to the preregistered rule (list, exclude, report a generator with more than 2 percent failures as failed), before the closed look. No closed clip failed, so this fix did not change any number. (3) e027_errors.py: output renamed to the preregistered error_analysis.json. Added (not in the preregistration's script list, written and run after the freeze and before the closed look, no choice depends on it): e027_dev_supp.py, which recomputes the selectable candidates' out-of-fold scores to store the simplicity-rule differences and LOGO minus LOFO for every selectable candidate (dev_supp.json). It reproduces dev_results.json to 4 decimals except three values that differ by 0.0001 (P8-IMG-07 LOGO 0.6872 against 0.6871, P8-IMG-17 LOGO 0.6621 against 0.6620, and one interval end of P8-IMG-08 LOFO); attributed to floating-point differences between the parallel workers of the first run and the single process of the second (lbfgs on multithreaded BLAS); no selection or verdict depends on the fourth decimal. A preflight of e027_closed.py was run in the scratch folder with 50 OPEN generated clips standing in for the closed rows (outputs discarded, no closed clip read) to make a crash after the marker unlikely.
