  - Comparison with E-020 C2 (closed 0.4880 [0.4534, 0.5204], against all 271 reals): the E-027 head reaches 0.7800 [0.7437, 0.8109] on the same 794 clips against the same 271 reals; on the 200-real primary set the paired difference to the installed head is +0.2618 [0.2155, 0.3080]. What changed between them is the training data (DeepAction in E-020, DF26 open generators here) and the encoder (DINOv2-small to CLIP ViT-L/14); with the E-020 encoder and DF26 training (P8-IMG-07), the open LOFO AUC is 0.6746, so both changes contribute, the training data more (E020 0.4995 to P8-IMG-07 0.6746 on the open subset; P8-IMG-07 to P8-IMG-13 +0.0707). Comparison with E-026: the confound E-026 measured for the E-020 head is also present for the E-027 deployable head (0.8023 against 0.7682 pooled false-positive rate, not a paired comparison).
  - Error analysis, open out-of-fold (fold seed 0, t_BA; error_analysis.json). False-negative rate per generator: HunyuanVideo i2v 0.4428 [0.3849, 0.5023], HunyuanVideo t2v 0.3395 [0.2857, 0.3978], LTX i2v 0.2768 [0.2269, 0.3329], LTX t2v 0.2251 [0.1794, 0.2785], Wan 2.2 i2v 0.3321 [0.2787, 0.3902], Wan 2.2 t2v 0.2288 [0.1828, 0.2824]. Real false-positive rate (mean score over the three family runs) 0.2472 [0.1996, 0.3019]; by source kind: TalkingCelebs 3 of 62 = 0.0484 [0.0166, 0.1329], YouTube (OpenVid / MAVOS-DD) 64 of 209 = 0.3062 [0.2477, 0.3717]; by scenario: Direct_to_Camera_Casual 0.34 [0.2546, 0.4372], Official_Statement 0.0986 [0.0486, 0.1898], Studio_Interview 0.26 [0.1840, 0.3537]; by speaking style: animated 0.3578 [0.2741, 0.4512], calm 0.1831, casual 0.2195, formal 0.12 [0.0562, 0.2380]; by camera: slight_movement 0.3061, static 0.2342 (intervals overlap). Closed (t_BA, closed_results.json): false-negative rate by scenario Kling Direct_to_Camera_Casual 0.4388 [0.3447, 0.5375] against Studio_Interview 0.29 [0.2101, 0.3854], other generators within 0.03 between scenarios; real false-positive rate Direct_to_Camera_Casual 0.44 [0.3467, 0.5377], Studio_Interview 0.36 [0.2727, 0.4576]; animated real clips 0.4902 [0.3953, 0.5858]. Reading: the false positives concentrate on YouTube-sourced, animated, casual-camera real clips, and are rare on TalkingCelebs clips; this is a second sign, inside DF26, that the head responds to the kind of real source as well as to generation. Counts only; no DF26 frame or clip was written into any document.
  - Prediction check (predictions were not measurements): best selectable LOFO 0.70 to 0.90: 0.7453 (inside). LOGO 0.02 to 0.10 above LOFO: +0.0075 (below the range; wrong). C3 0.55 to 0.70: 0.4921 (below; wrong). CLIP or SigLIP above DINOv2-small: CLIP above (+0.0707), SigLIP below (-0.0254); half right. PT14 - P14 interval containing 0: contains 0 for IMG-08 and IMG-17, lies below 0 for IMG-07 and IMG-13 (the predicted "no evidence of temporal use" holds; the predicted interval shape does not for two encoders). Closed AUC 0.55 to 0.75 and lower than open LOFO: 0.7548 (just above the range) and not lower than 0.7453 (wrong in direction, no paired test exists between them). Wan 2.6 the best closed generator: yes (0.8758). Confound false-positive rate well above the DF26 real rate: yes (0.8023 against 0.2841).
  - Limitations. (a) The real side of the closed look is not fresh: the same 271 DF26 real clips were scored out of fold during development, and the threshold, calibrator and selection used their open out-of-fold scores; only the 794 generated clips are new to the head, so the closed AUC measures unseen generators against reused reals (there is no other DF26 real pool). (b) Second look at the closed subset, informed by the first (E-020). (c) The source-confound line says CONFOUND PERSISTS: on real footage outside DF26 the head flags 75 to 83 percent at t_BA, so a DF26 AUC is not a statement about real-world videos. (d) 218 source groups, not verified identities. (e) Three open families only. (f) 2.0 s, 7 fps, 224 px canonical clips; X4 suggests whole-clip sampling helps slightly. (g) Fold models (about four fifths of the data) produced the closed result, not the deployable model. (h) The CLIP ViT-L/14 weights carry CONDITIONAL licence wording (research use). (i) X4 has six comparisons without correction. (j) The calibrator was fitted at a 2:1 ratio and the closed ECE is at about 4:1.
  - Verdicts: H-X3 SUPPORTED (P8-IMG-13 0.7453 [0.7174, 0.7710] LOFO; +0.2532 [0.2259, 0.2811] over C3). H-T: no evidence that temporal features add to pooled appearance (PT14 - P14 -0.0404 [-0.0650, -0.0165] on IMG-13). Source confound: CONFOUND PERSISTS (pooled 0.8023 [0.7625, 0.8368]). X4: success line met by C16 P16 (+0.0188 [0.0060, 0.0321]). H-X3c SUPPORTED (0.7548 [0.7189, 0.7885]).
  - Consequence (decided autonomously within D-079 and D-081 - needs user review): nothing is installed; the product decision belongs to the main agent and the user. Under roadmap section 8.5 the frozen deployable head (deploy_P8-IMG-13.npz, 778cb271...5ce3) passed its own success criterion and is a candidate for an experimental head only (new head_id, new folder, gate closed, `APPROVED_HEADS` unchanged), with a tested-scope text that must quote the source-confound result; promotion to definitive findings would need an independent preregistered validation with fresh real clips, which this experiment does not provide.
  - Reproduction (from the repository root, PowerShell or Git Bash; PYTHONPATH="src;scripts/modern_video"; each step refuses to overwrite its result): `.venv/Scripts/python.exe scripts/modern_video/e027_prereg.py --hash-only` (only on a fresh folder), `e027_features.py IMG-07 IMG-08 IMG-17 IMG-13`, `e027_x4.py encode`, `e027_dev.py`, `e027_dev_supp.py`, `e027_confound.py`, `e027_x4.py features`, `e027_x4.py analyse`, `e027_errors.py`, `e027_closed.py --final` (refuses once closed_look.json exists). Runtime: features 476 s, X4 encode 167 s, dev 205 s (132 fit jobs in 185 s on 10 CPU workers), confound 64 s, X4 features 471 s, closed look 75 s; total compute about 30 minutes. Output SHA-256 (first 8 and last 4 hex): dev_results.json cb4f35b9...7739, dev_supp.json a6b771a0...faf2, oof_scores.csv 702abdd0...3971, groups_folds.csv ef834a21...5173, freeze.json ca43fcbe...143b, confound_results.json 7694286b...afa9, x4_results.json 24c02b7e...d529, closed_look.json 77867a71...579c, closed_results.json 1ec329b5...4891, closed_scores.csv 0688020a...4cab, error_analysis.json f269ea53...8888.
  - Independent check by the main agent, 2026-10-27 (not a new look: only the stored closed_scores.csv was read, no closed clip or feature): the preregistration span hash re-derives as stated; from closed_scores.csv the primary AUC recomputes to 0.7548 (n 994, 200 reals) and the all-real AUC to 0.7801 (stored 0.78), matching. The per-generator AUCs recomputed from the CSV differ from the stored values by 0.0003 to 0.0015 (for example Wan_2.6 0.8743 against 0.8758): the CSV rounds scores to 6 decimals, which ties 204 of 1,065 scores at 1.000000 (11 of them real clips), whereas closed_results.json was computed in memory at full precision. The stored values stand; anyone recomputing from the CSV should expect differences of this size.

## E-030: equal error rate of the speech task from the stored sealed score files (post-hoc metric, no model run, no new data read)   (registered 2026-10-27 before the metric was computed; decided autonomously - needs user review)
- Status: COMPLETED 2026-10-27 (registered wording: "Status: PLANNED (changed to COMPLETED when the result is appended)."; the registered block's SHA-256 b854de92...0e65 and time 15:14:09 +0530 are in data/_results/e030/prereg.json).
- Why: the literature on synthetic speech detection reports EER (ASVspoof 2019 LA, In-the-Wild), while E-014 reported AUC only, so docs/PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARK.md can only call the audio rows partially comparable (its open issue 3).
- What is read: only the score files that the E-014 freeze already wrote, data/_final/scores/<tag>.csv (hashes in their provenance files), rows with modality audio, dataset asvspoof2019_la split test_in and dataset in_the_wild split test_x, for the tags I-0, I-2, I-3 and I-4 (I-4 is the frozen speech system). No model is run, no sample is rescored, nothing is fitted, selected, calibrated or thresholded, and no file under data/_final or ledger is written. Rule 3 reading: this is a second metric on scores of an already frozen and already reported sealed evaluation; it cannot change any model or choice. It is labelled post hoc wherever it is quoted.
- Metric: EER by src/dfd/eval/metrics.eer (the project's own implementation) per seed; reported as the seed mean, the seed standard deviation, and a group bootstrap 95 percent interval (src/dfd/eval/bootstrap.cluster_bootstrap, metric "eer", groups = the group_id column, 2,000 resamples, seed 20261027, all seeds jointly as in E-014). The AUC is recomputed the same way and must equal the E-014 values to 4 decimals (gate; on failure nothing is reported except the mismatch).
- Expectation (not a measurement): I-4 EER on ASVspoof 2019 LA test a few percent, on In-the-Wild between 10 and 25 percent; ordering of tags as for AUC.
- Limitation in advance: EER differs from published values by protocol (our In-the-Wild test_x is a speaker-disjoint slice, D-035 style; our ASVspoof test_in follows the project split), so most rows stay partially comparable even with matched metrics.
- Result: (to be appended below after the run)
- E-030 RESULT, 2026-10-27 15:2x +0530 (script scripts/eval/e030_eer.py, SHA-256 36e633c7...e695; output data/_results/e030/e030_results.json, SHA-256 137bf45a...c4a2; read only the stored sealed score files; no model run; post hoc).
  - Gate: the recomputed AUCs equal E-014 to 4 decimals for every row (I-0 0.9684 and 0.7516; I-2 0.9632 and 0.8125; I-3 0.9541 and 0.8672; I-4 0.9835 and 0.9148): PASS.

E-030 reproduces the point estimates exactly; its intervals differ in the third decimal ([0.0595, 0.0782], [0.1269, 0.1883]) because E-030 drew its own bootstrap. The EER is therefore not a post-hoc metric of the sealed run: it was computed at the freeze and only not copied into this log. ## E-028: the frozen generated-image detector T-I on a test-only subset of Community Forensics-Eval (21 modern generators), with a face-image analysis for D8 and a format-shortcut control   (preregistered 2026-10-27, before any E-028 image was decoded or scored; decided autonomously within D-080 and D-081 - needs user review)
- Status: COMPLETED 2026-10-27 (result appended below; this line said PLANNED when the entry was registered, nothing else above the result was changed). Ordering record: commits are not authorised, so this block's SHA-256 (from the heading line to the first line equal to "- Result: (to be appended below after the run)", inclusive, UTF-8) and the time are written to data/_results/modern/e028/prereg.json by scripts/modern_image/prereg.py before the first image is decoded (weaker evidence than a commit; docs/RESEARCH_INTEGRITY.md section 2). The shard downloads started before this block was hashed, because the measured rate (about 3 to 4 MB/s) would otherwise not fit the time budget; downloading opens no image and computes no score (decided autonomously - needs user review).
- Question: how does the frozen T-I detector, exactly as shipped, do on images of 21 contemporary generators (commercial, latent diffusion, pixel diffusion, GAN, other) against their paired real images? How would it do on still images that contain a face, which the product now routes to the face-swap path (UX-7, decision D8 open)? Does a compression-format difference between real and generated images explain part of any separation?
- Model (read only, nothing fitted, selected or calibrated): checkpoints/I-5-freeze-candidate, checkpoint_id I-5-freeze-candidate-20261025-1608, task T-I: encoder IMG-13 openai/clip-vit-large-patch14 revision 32bd6428...cc41 (weights hash-verified in data/_logs/encoder_weights.json), preprocess_id ccbaba9519dc (whole image, the encoder model card's processor), linear head head_T-I.npz (SHA-256 42bbb3494bbf27b636abfd254a767e72822f0d0ce910a5e395272ab0d1ef9d2a), temperature 0.6359970431860519, threshold 0.2072609853917272 on the calibrated probability (maximum balanced accuracy on progan_val, D-024); metadata.json SHA-256 65844a4deef92672a7e562a7f55be104a9302ee20a167925e4d387be705a74ed. For the face-path analysis only, task T-V of the same checkpoint (fine-tuned SigLIP base, weights_T-V.pt SHA-256 e0c2154d5628eb8e5d8e75970ffe3f57f3009b589cc79fa67244a0d7d8250186, temperature 0.8321, threshold 0.6138830927259571).
- Data: subset I7-S of DATA_LOG (CommunityForensics-Eval revision 7d4a74a8...ea0, CC BY-NC-SA 4.0, test only): 92 shards, 11,550 rows (5,791 real, 5,759 fake), chosen by the written rule in data/cf_eval/_meta/subset.json before any image was seen; label 1 = fake, 0 = real (dataset card). NSFW-flagged rows are dropped (none in Eval). Cells: each generator's fakes against its own paired reals (LAION, COCO, ImageNet, FFHQ test split); Hourglass has two pairings (ImageNet and FFHQ), reported as two cells and pooled; the eight RAISE-paired generators (DeciDiffusionV2, GALIP, kandinsky_2_2, LCM_lora_sdv15, LCM_lora_sdxl, LCM_lora_ssd1b, kvikontent_midjourney_v6, stable_cascade) are each scored against the one pooled RAISE real cell (121 TIFF images from four pairings), because RAISE reals cost about 20 MB each; this departs from the card's per-generator pairing and is stated with every RAISE number.
- Scoring path (the product's, reused): image bytes decoded by cv2.imdecode(..., IMREAD_COLOR) as in RealAdapter.analyse; then the adapter's whole-image path: RGB, PIL image, the encoder's own processor (dfd.sweep.encoders.HFImageProcessor), Encoder.encode_pixels (fp16 autocast), the checkpoint's Head.prob_rows, and the temperature of real_adapter._calibrate. Scores are computed in batches with the same objects; parity check before any metric: 200 rows drawn with numpy default_rng(20261027) are rescored one at a time by RealAdapter._score_whole_image("T-I", bgr); if the largest absolute difference exceeds 0.02 (the tolerance of D-044) the batched scores are discarded and every row is scored one at a time. An image that cv2 cannot decode is listed and excluded (the product would fail on it too); if more than 2 percent of a cell fails, the cell is reported as failed.
- Metrics per cell, per architecture (Commercial, LatDiff, PixDiff, GAN, Other: that architecture's fakes against the union of their paired reals) and pooled (all fakes against all reals): ROC AUC (dfd.eval.metrics.roc_auc), PR-AUC (average precision, fake = positive), and at the frozen threshold: false-positive rate (reals flagged), false-negative rate (fakes missed), precision, recall, F1, balanced accuracy. Macro summary: mean over the 21 generators of the per-generator metric (Hourglass with both pairings pooled), as the card's mAP is a mean over generators. Intervals: 2,000 bootstrap resamples, resampling reals and fakes separately within each cell (images are the units; no person or source grouping exists in the metadata), 95 percent percentile, numpy seed 20261027; macro intervals resample every cell in the same draw. Precision depends on the about 1:1 class ratio of the subset and is stated with it.
- Face analysis (evidence for D8; routing is not changed): (F1) the Hourglass-FFHQ cell (FFHQ test-split portraits against Hourglass FFHQ fakes). (F2) Product routing: the adapter's own detector (YuNet, the T-V preprocess_config, RealAdapter._det) on the full decoded image; "face-routed" = at least one face. Reported: share face-routed by label, real source and generator; T-I metrics on face-routed fakes against face-routed reals (pooled, and per architecture where at least 30 images of each class are face-routed) and on the rest. (F3) What the product reports today for face-routed images: the T-V face-crop score of the same checkpoint through the adapter's own crop (largest face, enlarge 1.3, 224 px, JPEG 95 round trip, RealAdapter._score_rgb), with AUC, FPR and FNR at the T-V threshold, and the paired bootstrap difference of AUC, T-I minus T-V, on the same face-routed images.
- Format and resolution control: (C1) format composition per cell from the metadata; AUC of a format-only score (1 for PNG, 0 otherwise) per cell, to show how much a pure format cue could separate; T-I AUC restricted to PNG reals against PNG fakes (pooled), and to JPEG reals against JPEG fakes (Firefly cells). (C2) Re-encoding control: a fixed sample of PNG fakes (up to 20 per generator, numpy default_rng(20261027)) is re-encoded in memory to JPEG quality 90 (cv2.imencode) and rescored; reported: median paired score change, FNR at the threshold before and after, and per-architecture AUC against the same reals with the original and with the re-encoded fakes (paired bootstrap of the difference). (C3) AUC of log pixel count alone per cell, as a size-cue reference.
- Lines (fixed now), for the product question "is T-I adequate as a modern-generator image check": ADEQUATE if the macro AUC lower bound is at least 0.85 and the pooled FPR upper bound is at most 0.10 and the pooled FNR upper bound is at most 0.30; INADEQUATE if the macro AUC upper bound is below 0.75, or the pooled FPR lower bound is above 0.20, or the pooled FNR lower bound is above 0.50; otherwise MIXED. Faces (D8): "T-I better than the current face path on face images" if the paired AUC difference T-I minus T-V on face-routed images has its lower bound above 0, "worse" if the upper bound is below 0, otherwise "not distinguished"; "T-I usable on face images" only if the face-routed AUC lower bound is at least 0.85 and the face-routed FPR upper bound is at most 0.10.
- Predictions (not measurements): macro AUC 0.50 to 0.70 (basis: the CommunityForensics-Eval card reports mAP 0.583 on this set for "Ojha et al.", which is a CLIP ViT-L/14 linear probe trained on ProGAN, the same recipe as T-I; this is the card's number, quoted, not a project measurement); higher AUC on the GAN cells (DFGAN, GALIP) than on the commercial ones; FNR above 0.5 on most commercial generators; Hourglass-FFHQ AUC below 0.80; T-V on face-routed images near chance; a sizeable format-only AUC for most cells and an increase of FNR after JPEG re-encoding. INADEQUATE is the expected verdict.
- Outputs (new folders only): scripts/modern_image/ (prereg.py, extract.py, e028.py, lib.py), data/_results/modern/e028/ (prereg.json, features and scores cache, parity.json, e028_results.json, e028_cells.csv, logs). Nothing is installed; no checkpoint, threshold, routing, gate or APPROVED_HEADS changes. No image goes into any document.
- Known limitations in advance: 200 to 800 images per cell (intervals of about plus or minus 0.03 to 0.05 in AUC); pooled RAISE reals (121) for eight generators; the subset was chosen by cost per byte, so within a generator the rows of the cheapest shards are over-represented (the shards hold contiguous rows of one generator; no content-based choice was made); the card's reals and fakes are paired by source dataset and prompt, not by content, so a cell's AUC mixes generation artefacts with content and format differences, which C1 to C3 only partly separate; face-routed counts depend on YuNet's recall on generated faces; the Eval set says nothing about images edited after generation or shared through social media.
- Result: (to be appended below after the run)

  - Per generator (AUC [95%]; FNR at the threshold): DFGAN 0.9999 [0.9995, 1.0000], 0.004; Hourglass-ImageNet 0.9129 [0.8916, 0.9329], 0.676; Dalle2 0.8604 [0.8323, 0.8865], 0.708; GALIP 0.8372 [0.7992, 0.8738], 0.315; Imagen3 0.7691 [0.7352, 0.8020], 0.872; Dalle3 0.7330 [0.6947, 0.7701], 0.918; MidjourneyV6_1 0.7327 [0.6955, 0.7720], 0.941; LCM_lora_sdxl 0.7316 [0.6803, 0.7842], 0.548; Firefly_Image3 0.7315 [0.6879, 0.7720], 0.902; MidjourneyV5_2 0.7263 [0.6885, 0.7677], 0.869; Hourglass-FFHQ 0.7217 [0.6755, 0.7661], 0.607; IdeogramV1 0.7170 [0.6802, 0.7516], 0.906; FLUX-schnell 0.7022 [0.6628, 0.7430], 0.885; Firefly_Image2 0.6976 [0.6520, 0.7400], 0.921; IdeogramV2 0.6958 [0.6570, 0.7343], 0.947; FLUX-dev 0.6629 [0.6168, 0.7079], 0.923; kvikontent_midjourney_v6 0.6198 [0.5626, 0.6771], 0.729; LCM_lora_ssd1b 0.6186 [0.5613, 0.6801], 0.706; stable_cascade 0.5995 [0.5374, 0.6600], 0.748; LCM_lora_sdv15 0.5965 [0.5406, 0.6548], 0.765; DeciDiffusionV2 0.4951 [0.4354, 0.5573], 0.844; kandinsky_2_2 0.4145 [0.3536, 0.4768], 0.893. The eight RAISE-paired generators share one pooled real cell of 121 RAISE TIFF photographs, whose FPR is 0.157 [0.091, 0.223]. The full per-cell table with PR-AUC, precision, recall and F1 is e028_cells.csv.
  - Lines: INADEQUATE (macro AUC upper bound 0.7229 is below 0.75, and the pooled FNR lower bound 0.7300 is above 0.50). Prediction check (predictions were not measurements): macro AUC predicted 0.50 to 0.70, observed 0.704 (just above the range); GAN above commercial (as predicted); FNR above 0.5 on every commercial generator (as predicted, 0.71 to 0.95); Hourglass-FFHQ below 0.80 (as predicted, 0.722); T-V near chance on face-routed images (0.602, slightly above chance).
  - Face analysis for D8. (F2) The product's YuNet routing finds at least one face in 26.3 percent of the real images (LAION 23.3, COCO 36.5, ImageNet 17.5, RAISE 17.4, FFHQ 100 percent) and in 27.7 percent of the fakes (from 10.5 percent for kvikontent_midjourney_v6 to 56.9 percent for Hourglass); every face-routed image produced a face crop. On the 3,119 face-routed images (1,595 fake, 1,524 real) T-I would reach AUC 0.7859 [0.7717, 0.7995], FPR 0.0269 [0.0195, 0.0347], FNR 0.8063 [0.7885, 0.8254]; on the 8,431 others AUC 0.8040 [0.7953, 0.8129], FPR 0.0471, FNR 0.7140. (F3) What the product reports today for those images, the T-V face-crop score at its threshold 0.6139: AUC 0.6020 [0.5836, 0.6202], FPR 0.2152 [0.1949, 0.2350], FNR 0.6621 [0.6406, 0.6849]. Paired, T-I minus T-V on the same face-routed images: AUC +0.1839 [+0.1596, +0.2074], FPR -0.1883 [-0.2093, -0.1660], FNR +0.1442 [+0.1172, +0.1721]. Per architecture the AUC difference is positive for Commercial (+0.199 [0.166, 0.233]), GAN (+0.230 [0.169, 0.296]) and PixDiff (+0.242 [0.187, 0.300]) and negative for LatDiff (-0.146 [-0.280, -0.015]) and Other (-0.227 [-0.404, -0.046]), whose face-routed real cell has only 21 RAISE images. (F1) Hourglass-FFHQ portraits: T-I AUC 0.7217 [0.6755, 0.7661], FPR 0.1304, FNR 0.6069; T-V AUC 0.5141 [0.4648, 0.5642]; difference +0.2076 [+0.1436, +0.2696]. Lines: "T-I better than the current face path on face images": met (lower bound +0.1596 above 0); "T-I usable on face images": not met (face-routed AUC lower bound 0.7717 below 0.85; FNR 0.81). Both scorers miss most generated faces at their thresholds.
  - Format and size controls. (C1) A format-only score (1 for PNG) separates the cells with macro AUC 0.9301 [0.9284, 0.9318] (pooled 0.9222): Eval fakes are 92.8 percent PNG (5,342 of 5,759; the 417 JPEG fakes are all Firefly) and Eval reals 88.1 percent JPEG (5,101 of 5,791; also 482 PNG, 121 TIFF, 68 WEBP, 19 GIF). Restricted to PNG reals against PNG fakes, T-I's pooled AUC is 0.6123 [0.5908, 0.6353] (482 reals, mostly FFHQ), against 0.7973 for all images; JPEG only (the Firefly fakes against JPEG reals) 0.7456 [0.7252, 0.7668]. Image size alone (log pixel count) also separates many commercial cells (AUC 0.88 to 0.99 for Dalle, Ideogram, Imagen, Midjourney and Firefly), so format and size are both confounded with the label in this set. (C2) Re-encoding 380 sampled PNG fakes to JPEG quality 90 lowered 87.1 percent of their scores, raised their FNR from 0.729 to 0.816 (+0.087 [0.058, 0.116]) and changed the AUC against the same reals by -0.059 [-0.067, -0.051] pooled (Commercial -0.066, LatDiff -0.088, PixDiff -0.126, Other -0.149; GAN +0.004 [-0.014, 0.023]). Reading: part of T-I's separation is a compression cue (PNG looks more generated to it), not only generation evidence. (C3) Errors: FPR by real format JPEG 0.028, PNG 0.120, TIFF 0.157, GIF 19 of 19 (17 real images are smaller than 32 px, mostly GIFs); FNR by fake resolution 224 and 256 px 0.31 and 0.38 (GAN and Hourglass), 512 to 2048 px 0.79 to 0.91.
  - Score distribution: median calibrated score 0.0001 for reals and 0.0175 for fakes, and 75 percent of fakes score below 0.233, so most modern fakes fall below a threshold that was chosen on ProGAN validation images.
  - Reading. Measured: the shipped T-I head ranks Eval fakes above reals better than chance overall (pooled AUC 0.797) but misses about three quarters of modern generated images at its frozen threshold (pooled FNR 0.74; 0.71 to 0.95 on the commercial generators); it is at chance on DeciDiffusionV2 and below chance on kandinsky_2_2 (both against RAISE reals); it detects the GAN images (0.958). Part of its separation follows the PNG against JPEG difference between fakes and reals. On face images the current face-swap path is worse than T-I would be (paired AUC difference +0.18), but neither reaches a usable level on modern generated faces. Not shown: anything about images after social-media processing or editing.
  - Consequence (for the main agent; nothing changed here): T-I remains a ProGAN-era detector and its scope text can cite E-028. D8 evidence: routing face images to T-I instead of T-V would raise AUC and lower FPR on this set, at a higher FNR, and would still miss most generated faces; this supports at most an experimental whole-image check on face images, not a finding.
  - Independent check by the main agent, 2026-10-27 evening (no new look, nothing rescored: only the stored per-image scores of data/_results/modern/e028/cache/*.npz joined to data/cf_eval/_meta/index.csv were read, with a separate implementation written for the check: scikit-learn roc_auc_score, own rate and bootstrap code). Rows: 11,550, all keys unique, 5,759 fake and 5,791 real, equal to the subset index; thresholds equal to the checkpoint metadata (0.2072609853917272; T-V 0.6138830927259571). Recomputed and equal to the stored values: all 21 per-generator AUCs to 4 decimals (21 of 21, Hourglass with both real pairings pooled and the eight RAISE-paired generators against the 121 RAISE reals), the macro AUC 0.7039, the pooled AUC 0.7973, pooled FNR 0.7395 and FPR 0.0418, the mean per-generator FNR 0.7613. Face analysis: 3,119 face-routed images (1,595 fake, 1,524 real), T-I AUC 0.7859, T-V AUC 0.6020, difference +0.1839; an independent stratified bootstrap of the difference (1,000 resamples, seed 1) gave [0.1608, 0.2071] against the stored [0.1596, 0.2074]. Format: format-only pooled AUC 0.9222, T-I AUC on PNG images only 0.6123, fake PNG share 0.9276, real JPEG share 0.8808. Definitions confirmed from the code: the macro AUC (0.7039) is the mean of the 21 per-generator AUCs, the pooled AUC (0.7973) ranks all fakes against all reals, and the pooled miss rate (0.7395, the "74 percent") is the share of all 5,759 fakes below the frozen threshold; they are different quantities and both are reported. Preregistration: the block hash re-derives to 420c5b1ce351996617ff8153bb0dff9e577ae0b267579012f70a4f7c67cb878a (9,303 characters, equal to prereg.json) when the Status line is put back to the PLANNED wording of E-029's registered line; the only change above the result is that line. Not independently rechecked: the parity rescoring (needs the GPU path; its largest difference 0.0030 is in parity.json) and the re-encoding control (needs the images). Discrepancies with the hand-over: none in the numbers.

## E-029: a new linear head on the frozen T-I encoder trained on Community Forensics-Small, validated on held-out generators, evaluated once on the E-028 Eval subset, paired against frozen T-I   (preregistered 2026-10-27, before any E-029 feature was extracted or any head was fitted; decided autonomously within D-080 (b) and D-081 - needs user review)
- Status: COMPLETED 2026-10-27 21:08 (result appended below; nothing else above the result was changed). Registered wording of this sentence, kept so the preregistration hash can be re-checked by putting it back: "Status: PLANNED (this line is changed to COMPLETED when the result is appended; nothing else above the result will change)." Ordering record: as E-028, this block's SHA-256 and the time are written to data/_results/modern/e029/prereg.json by scripts/modern_image/prereg.py before any E-029 feature is extracted.
- Question: does training the same kind of head (frozen encoder, linear probe) on many contemporary generators instead of ProGAN improve detection of 21 generators that are not in its training data, and at what false-positive rate on real images from sources it has not seen? Is any gain explained by a compression-format shortcut?
- Data. Training and validation: subset I6-S of DATA_LOG (CommunityForensics-Small revision 6c539a53...5278, CC BY-NC-SA 4.0, train split only; 15 shards). Evaluation: the E-028 rows of I7-S, once, after the freeze. NSFW-flagged rows are dropped. At most 1,000 images per (model_name, real_source) enter training and at most 1,000 enter validation, drawn with numpy default_rng(20261027) before any feature is read.
- Validation split (not a random image split). Fakes are split by generator: within each architecture with at least 3 generators in the subset, the model_names are sorted by SHA-256 of the name and every fifth (positions 0, 5, 10, ...) goes to validation; architectures with fewer than 3 generators stay in training only. Reals are split by source item: COCO and FFHQ images by SHA-256 of the file name (first 8 hex digits as an integer, mod 5 equal to 0 goes to validation); VISION images by device (the D-number prefix of the file name), with the same hash rule on the device id, so no device is on both sides. The split is written to data/_results/modern/e029/split.csv before any feature is read.
- Encoders and features: primary IMG-13 (the T-I encoder; the same whole-image features as E-028, the same product path); secondary IMG-15 laion/CLIP-ViT-L-14-laion2B-s32B-b82K revision 16270321...d07a (on disk, hash-verified, no download), whose model-card processor has the same resize and crop as IMG-13 and differs only in mean and standard deviation, so its input is the IMG-13 processor output re-normalised exactly. Features are extracted once and cached in data/_results/modern/e029/.
- Head and calibration (the T-I recipe, reused): dfd.sweep.heads.train_head (standardisation on training rows, one linear layer, class-balanced BCE, AdamW 1e-3, weight decay 1e-4, batch 1,024, at most 200 epochs, early stopping on validation AUC with patience 10), seeds 0, 1 and 2 per encoder; temperature scaling fitted on validation (dfd.eval.calibration.fit_temperature on the head's logits), and the threshold of maximum balanced accuracy on the calibrated validation probabilities (dfd.eval.threshold.choose_threshold_max_balanced_accuracy), per seed, as in scripts/mvp/train_mvp.py. Validation is used for early stopping, temperature, threshold and the encoder choice; nothing is chosen on Eval.
- Encoder choice (validation only, fixed now): the encoder with the higher seed-mean validation AUC; if the paired bootstrap interval of the validation difference contains 0, IMG-13 (the product's encoder, so a change of the head file alone). The chosen encoder's seed-0 head is the "frozen candidate"; all six heads are evaluated once on Eval and reported.
- Freeze: checkpoints/modern-E029/ (new folder): six heads as .npz (mean, std, w, b, the format of dfd.infer.checkpoint.Head), meta.json (encoders, revisions, data, split hash, seeds, temperatures, thresholds, validation metrics, the chosen encoder), freeze.json with the SHA-256 of every file. The Eval script refuses to run if any hash differs or if data/_results/modern/e029/eval_look.json exists (one look).
- Generator overlap (measured, reported before the Eval look): exact model_name overlap between the training generators and the 21 Eval generators; "related base model in training" proxy: an Eval generator counts as related if a training model_name contains one of these lower-case keywords: LCM_lora_sdv15: "stable-diffusion-v1-5", "sd-v1-5", "sd15", "v1-5"; LCM_lora_sdxl and kvikontent_midjourney_v6: "stable-diffusion-xl", "sdxl"; LCM_lora_ssd1b: "ssd-1b"; kandinsky_2_2: "kandinsky"; stable_cascade: "cascade", "wuerstchen"; DeciDiffusionV2: "decidiffusion"; FLUX-dev and FLUX-schnell: "flux"; Hourglass: "hourglass"; DFGAN: "dfgan", "df-gan"; GALIP: "galip"; Dalle2 and Dalle3: "dall-e", "dalle-2", "dalle-3"; Midjourney: "midjourney"; the others (Firefly, Ideogram, Imagen3): none. The proxy is coarse (a name says nothing certain about weights) and is labelled so. Results are reported separately for exact-seen, related and unrelated generators.
- Metrics on Eval: as E-028 (per cell, per architecture, pooled, macro), at each head's own validation threshold. Primary comparison: macro AUC of the IMG-13 head minus that of frozen T-I on the same images, paired bootstrap (same resamples for both, as E-028), per seed and for the seed mean (each resample computes the metric for the three seeds and averages). Seed mean, seed standard deviation and the bootstrap interval of the seed mean are reported for every headline metric (CLAUDE.md rule 4). The same analyses as E-028 are repeated for the head: face cell F1 and face-routed images F2 (with the paired difference to T-I and to the T-V face path), format control C1 and C2 (the re-encoded fakes of E-028), real source and resolution.
- Lines (fixed now). H-29 (improvement over T-I) SUPPORTED if the seed-mean paired difference in macro AUC (IMG-13 head minus T-I) has its lower bound above 0 and each seed's lower bound is above 0; NOT SUPPORTED if the seed-mean interval contains 0 or lies below it. Product eligibility of the frozen candidate as a candidate experimental image check under roadmap section 8.5 (never an approval): ELIGIBLE only if its macro AUC lower bound is at least 0.85, its pooled FPR upper bound at its validation threshold is at most 0.10, and the JPEG re-encoding control lowers its per-architecture AUC by less than 0.10 (point) in every architecture; otherwise "keep out". Unseen generalisation is reported for the unrelated generators separately; no claim beyond those intervals.
- Predictions (not measurements): validation AUC on held-out generators 0.93 to 0.99; Eval macro AUC 0.70 to 0.90 for the IMG-13 head, H-29 supported; the gain is largest on latent-diffusion cells and smallest on the commercial ones; the JPEG-compressed Firefly fakes get a high FNR because most training reals are JPEG and most training fakes PNG (format shortcut); FPR on LAION and RAISE reals is higher than on FFHQ and COCO reals (unseen real sources); IMG-15 similar to IMG-13 on validation (difference interval containing 0, so IMG-13 is chosen); not ELIGIBLE is somewhat more likely than ELIGIBLE because of the threshold transfer and the format control.
- Exclusions and stop rules: as E-028 for undecodable images; if fewer than 5,000 training images remain after the cap, or the download of the Small subset is incomplete when the time budget ends, E-029 runs on the shards present, which are listed in the result (a reduced run, labelled so). A failed run stays in the log.
- Outputs (new folders only): scripts/modern_image/ (e029_split.py, e029_train.py, e029_eval.py, lib.py), data/_results/modern/e029/ (prereg.json, split.csv, feature caches, val_results.json, overlap.json, eval_look.json, e029_results.json, e029_cells.csv, logs), checkpoints/modern-E029/. Nothing is installed into the product; APPROVED_HEADS, thresholds and routing are unchanged.
- Known limitations in advance: a 15-shard subset of Small (44,881 rows: 32,913 fake from 156 of the 4,782 generator names, 11,968 real of which 8,976 JPEG and 2,992 PNG; 124 rows NSFW-flagged; 2 of the 66 Systematic latent-diffusion shards); the real sources of Small (COCO, FFHQ, VISION) differ from those of Eval (LAION, RAISE, COCO, ImageNet, FFHQ), so Eval FPR mixes real-source shift with detection; a linear probe on a frozen 224 px CLIP feature cannot use high-frequency detail removed by the resize; validation generators come from the same subset families as training (Systematic Hugging Face models are mostly Stable Diffusion derivatives), so validation is optimistic for truly new architectures; one Eval look per head.
- Result: (to be appended below after the run)
- Amendment A1 (2026-10-27 15:07 +0530, after the split rule was run on the metadata index and before any E-029 feature was extracted or any head fitted; decided autonomously - needs user review): the cap of at most 1,000 rows per (model_name, real_source) and split applies to fake rows only. Applied literally, it also capped each real source (the reals carry one model_name per source: COCO, FFHQ, VISION) at 1,000 per split and would have discarded 7,362 of the 11,968 real rows; the cap was meant to stop single generators from dominating. Measured by the split script before the amendment, and unchanged by it: 125 training and 31 validation generators; no Eval generator name occurs exactly among the training generators; the related-base proxy matches LCM_lora_sdv15, LCM_lora_sdxl and kvikontent_midjourney_v6 only; the 6 VISION devices of the subset (D03, D05, D06, D18, D23, D35) all hash to training, so validation has COCO and FFHQ reals only.
- E-029 RESULT, 2026-10-27 (scripts/modern_image/: extract.py, e029_split.py, e029_train.py, e029_eval.py; features cached 15:07 to 21:07 +0530, heads fitted and frozen 21:08:04, one Eval look 21:08:12; outputs data/_results/modern/e029/ and checkpoints/modern-E029/, both git-ignored; environment ENV-DESKTOP-GPU: Windows 10 19045, Python 3.13.15, torch 2.14.1+cu130, scikit-learn 1.9.1, RTX 5070 12,227 MiB). Nothing was chosen on Eval; Eval was read once, after the freeze, by the frozen heads only. Full run, not reduced: all 15 Small shards used.
  - Interruption and deviations (all recorded, none changes the protocol text above). (1) The run was interrupted at 15:45 (7 of 15 shards complete) and resumed at 19:23; the earlier session's feature-cache loop (extract_loop.sh) was still alive and was left running; it only extracts shards whose SHA-256 matched and skips finished ones (reencode_sample.json hash unchanged, 38e00605...3f89). (2) The Hugging Face client did not reuse the old partial files (new partial names), so the 8 missing shards were fetched again: 3 by scripts/modern_image/download.py (4 threads, about 0.3 MB/s per connection) and the last 5 (72, 116, 64, 0, 94) by a new script, scripts/modern_image/download_ranged.py (HTTP Range chunks of 32 MiB, 16 connections, same pinned revision, same SHA-256 check against the Hub value before a shard counts, same log file). The total rate stayed at 1.4 to 2.4 MB/s, so the parallel script did not make the link faster; both methods log the same fields (the ranged records add "method"). All 15 records match the Hub SHA-256 and the byte sizes of subset.json (checked again at 21:07 after the last shard). (3) Bug in e029_train.py found before any head was fitted: the loader read z["f13"][k] inside a loop, which re-reads the whole array from the npz for every row (quadratic time, then a memory error); fixed by loading each array once per file (3 lines; no protocol text, split, recipe, seed or threshold rule changed). (4) Dry runs, disclosed because they produced numbers: the training script with --dry on the 7 shards then present, scratch folder only (validation AUC about 0.99 for IMG-13 and 0.95 for IMG-15; discarded, never used for any choice, the real run re-fitted everything on all 15 shards), and a code test of e029_eval.py on scratch paths with six RANDOM heads (not trained models; it reproduced the E-028 T-I macro AUC 0.7039 and wrote nothing to the real folders). The real eval_look.json did not exist until 21:08:12. No Eval score of any trained head existed before the freeze.
  - Leakage checks (before the freeze). Training and validation share no (shard, row) pair and no real image name; they share no fake generator (125 training, 31 validation, 0 in both); no real image name of Small occurs in Eval; no Eval generator name occurs among the training generators (the three related-base proxy matches remain: LCM_lora_sdv15, LCM_lora_sdxl, kvikontent_midjourney_v6). Fake image names such as 00000078.png occur in more than one split and in Eval (707 between training and validation, 648 against Eval), but they are generic sequential names of different generators, not shared images; the real images carry source file names and have no overlap. split.csv hash equals meta.json.

Unrelated (18 generators): head 0.8805 [0.8716, 0.8892] against T-I 0.7130, difference +0.1675 [0.1540, 0.1803]. The gain is present, and smaller, on generators without a related base model in training.
  - Format controls. PNG real against PNG generated images only: head 0.8365 (T-I 0.6123); JPEG only: head 0.9053 (T-I 0.7456), so the head separates beyond the format shortcut. Re-encoding the 380 sampled PNG fakes to JPEG quality 90 (the E-028 sample): pooled AUC 0.8749 to 0.8021, change -0.0729 [-0.0827, -0.0643]; GAN -0.0173, commercial -0.0659, other -0.0583, latent diffusion -0.0809, pixel diffusion -0.1086 [-0.1505, -0.0735]; FNR of the sample 0.0447 to 0.0842. The JPEG-compressed Firefly fakes were not missed (false-negative rate of JPEG fakes 0.0216, of PNG fakes 0.0571).
  - Faces (face-routed images, 3,119). Head AUC 0.8575 [0.8458, 0.8683], FPR 0.3399, FNR 0.1480 (not face-routed: FPR 0.5861, FNR 0.0187). Paired AUC difference: head minus T-I +0.0716 [0.0572, 0.0861]; head minus the face-swap path (T-V face crop) +0.2555 [0.2341, 0.2765]. The "usable on face images" line of E-028 would not be met (lower bound 0.8458 below 0.85, FPR 0.34).
  - Lines. H-29: SUPPORTED. Product eligibility of the frozen candidate under roadmap section 8.5: NOT ELIGIBLE, keep out: the macro AUC lower bound 0.8756 meets 0.85, but the pooled FPR upper bound 0.5339 is far above 0.10, and the JPEG re-encoding control lowers the pixel-diffusion AUC by 0.1086 (not below 0.10); the other architectures are below 0.10.
  - Not selected, reported as the preregistration requires (all six heads were evaluated once): IMG-15 heads macro AUC 0.9222 / 0.9209 / 0.8994, seed mean 0.9142 (SD 0.0128) [0.9022, 0.9258], +0.2102 [0.1921, 0.2284] over T-I; pooled FPR (seed mean) 0.2967 [0.2862, 0.3074], FNR 0.0484. Post hoc observation, no choice made or changed: the encoder that validation ranked lower scored higher on Eval. The encoder rule was fixed in advance on validation only and was applied as written.
  - Prediction check (predictions were not measurements). Validation AUC on held-out generators 0.93 to 0.99: IMG-13 0.9823 (inside), IMG-15 0.9333 (inside). Eval macro AUC 0.70 to 0.90 and H-29 supported: 0.8839 and supported (inside). Gain largest on latent diffusion: yes (+0.32). Smallest on commercial: wrong (smallest on pixel diffusion and GANs; commercial +0.12). JPEG Firefly fakes get a high FNR: wrong (0.0216 for JPEG fakes). FPR on LAION and RAISE higher than on FFHQ and COCO: yes (0.59 and 0.50 against 0.15 and 0.03). IMG-15 similar to IMG-13 on validation (interval containing 0): wrong (IMG-13 higher, upper bound -0.0025). NOT ELIGIBLE somewhat more likely: yes.
  - Reading. Measured: training on many recent generators raised the ranking of the same kind of head from macro AUC 0.704 to 0.884 on 21 unseen generators (all three seeds), most on latent diffusion, with part of the gain also present on generators with no related training model and a gain that survives restriction to PNG or to JPEG images; but its threshold, chosen on validation reals from two sources, flags about half of the real Eval images (59 percent of LAION photographs), so as a detector at that threshold it is not usable, and the AUC gain is a ranking improvement only. The gain on pixel diffusion is nil and JPEG recompression lowers the AUC by 0.07 pooled. Not shown: anything about images that were edited or shared through social media, about real sources outside these five, or about a threshold calibrated on diverse reals (not tried, because choosing one on Eval would be tuning on the test set).
  - Limitations. (a) The training set is a 15-shard subset of Small (125 generators of 4,782 names); (b) validation reals are COCO and FFHQ only, so the threshold and the temperature carry that bias; (c) the generator-overlap proxy is by name and coarse; (d) the Eval subset is cost-selected and 82.5 percent of its reals are LAION; (e) Eval reals and fakes differ in format and size (E-028), only partly separated by the controls; (f) a linear probe on 224 px features; (g) one Eval look; (h) the same Eval images informed no choice, but E-028 had already shown them with T-I.
  - Consequence (decided autonomously within D-080 and D-081 - needs user review): nothing is installed; no checkpoint, threshold, routing, gate or APPROVED_HEADS changed; the E-029 heads stay research artefacts (checkpoints/modern-E029). The product keeps the frozen T-I head as an experimental whole-image check with no finding (D-083). A head of this kind could become a candidate only after a new preregistered experiment that calibrates its threshold on diverse real sources and passes a false-positive line on real sources it was not calibrated on.
  - Independent check by the main agent, 2026-10-27 evening (stored features and the frozen head files only, own numpy and scikit-learn code, no rescoring by the project's evaluation code): freeze.json hashes equal the files; split.csv hash equals meta.json; the look marker (21:08:12) is after the freeze (21:08:04); macro AUC of IMG-13 seeds 0, 1, 2 recomputes to 0.8863, 0.8832, 0.8823 and all 21 per-generator AUCs of seed 0 equal the stored values to 4 decimals; pooled AUC 0.8692, FPR 0.5213, FNR 0.0545 and the five real-source false-positive rates (LAION 0.5885, RAISE 0.4959, COCO 0.0328, FFHQ 0.1522, ImageNet 0.2488) recompute exactly. Preregistration: the block hash re-derives to 16ac35d74126e6efb1a4b0230bc55c31a2c2c8200b07fde59d496feaf094666c (with the Status line restored) and the amendment line to b8fbcf94...b46a as recorded.
  - Reproduction (repository root, PYTHONPATH=src): `.venv/Scripts/python.exe scripts/modern_image/download.py small 4` or `download_ranged.py small 16`, `extract.py small 6` (and `extract.py eval 6` for the Eval caches of E-028), `e029_split.py` (already run), `e029_train.py`, `e029_eval.py --final` (refuses a second look). Output SHA-256 (first 8 and last 4 hex): e029_results.json 1800b02e...3847, e029_cells.csv 9d26721c...a95d, val_results.json fee0eb21...c63d, split.csv f44c079f...1974, overlap.json ae8f8ec8...6ab0, eval_look.json 16dc5cac...11b4, train_log.txt 4d21a3b1...24a4, eval_log.txt ec74e1ad...9362.

## E-031: Gemini-generated image detection with format-controlled training (PROPOSED 2026-10-27; NOT preregistered, NOT run)
- Status: PROPOSED. Blocked before any data is acquired: (1) the licence and terms for using Gemini outputs to train and evaluate a detector are not confirmed (the user reads Google's primary terms); (2) no candidate dataset records prompts, generation dates or model build (bitmind/nano-banana: 9,457 Gemini 2.5 Flash Image Preview PNG files, uploader MIT tag, no prompts, no real images; zr-zhang MLLM set: research-only, generator per image not stated); (3) no matched real controls. No image was downloaded, no feature extracted, no model trained.
- Proposal, data requirements, controls (identical resize and JPEG re-encoding of both classes, format-only and size-only baselines, exact and near-duplicate audit across splits), compared models (A frozen T-I, B frozen E-029 candidate, C the E-029 recipe on Gemini development data, three seeds), the threshold rule fixed in advance (pooled validation FPR at most 0.02 on three real sources) and the success and failure lines: docs/GEMINI_FEASIBILITY.md section 4. The text becomes the preregistration (hashed before any image is decoded) only after blockers 1 to 3 are resolved.
- Nearest existing evidence (not Gemini): Google Imagen 3 in E-028 and E-029 (frozen T-I AUC 0.7691, missed 0.8719; E-029 head AUC 0.8646, flagged 0.5654 of the paired real images).

## E-032: false-positive rates of the frozen video threshold on real videos of the Celeb-DF family and DFD (post hoc, re-analysis of sealed scores already in the ledger, not confirmatory)   (computed 2026-10-27, logged 2026-10-28; not registered beforehand; decided autonomously - needs user review)
- Status: COMPLETED. Logged on 2026-10-28 because the two rates are quoted in the paper, the report, the slides and the face-video verdict wording (D-087 addendum) and had no E-entry. Rule 2 disclosure: no hypothesis, success criterion or failure line was written before the computation; the numbers were computed by the benchmark audit of 2026-10-27 (docs/PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARK.md section 12.4) and are labelled post hoc wherever they are quoted.
- Label: post hoc, re-analysis of sealed scores already in the ledger, not confirmatory. It changes no model, threshold, split or choice, and nothing may be selected or approved on it.
- What is read: data/_final/scores/I-4.csv (SHA-256 e0c78a8cd1b1f19a23cb3e82cdffba65979cfb4c368e75527c6eea09c79f383f, equal to the value in docs/PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARK.md section 12.4) and data/_final/eval/I-4.json (the per-seed thresholds selected on validation data at the freeze), rows with split other than val. The sealed blocks celebdf_v2 test_x, celebdf_pp test_x and dfd test_x of the I-4 evaluation are already in the ledger (E-014). No model is run, no sample is rescored, nothing is fitted or tuned, and nothing under data/_final or ledger is written (ledger SHA-256 0f60d7eb7e241d461f4ecf15d4be3717797fdaff5bdd1fa7144f907534ea52d4 before and after).
- Metric: false-positive rate = share of the real videos (label 0) whose score is at or above the frozen per-seed threshold, per seed, then the mean over the 3 seeds. The Celeb-DF family block is the Celeb-DF v2 test videos plus the Celeb-DF++ fakes against the same real videos, as in E-014, so its real videos are those of Celeb-DF v2.
- Script and command: scripts/eval/benchmark_audit_2026-10-27/frozen_rates.py (SHA-256 8636bac2edf04c16625652e84efd65156d1ec2592f8a16c6eb8fe8092f892a15), run as `.venv/Scripts/python.exe scripts/eval/benchmark_audit_2026-10-27/frozen_rates.py I-4 ffpp:test_in celebdf_v2:test_x celebdf_family:test_x celebdf_pp_with_v2_real:test_x dfd:test_x asvspoof2019_la:test_in in_the_wild:test_x forensynths_progan_test:test_in forensynths_cnn_subset:test_x diffusion_test:test_x`.
- Output files: (a) scripts/eval/benchmark_audit_2026-10-27/frozen_rates_output.txt, SHA-256 60d549288451b93888863552630c9c1d205a2dfc053ec7d93f95608f81e2633a (the file as saved on 2026-10-27; each line is cut at 170 characters, which removes only the trailing list of run identifiers); (b) docs/test_evidence/2026-10-28_phase_b/frozen_rates_I4_replay.txt, SHA-256 46e012d6e207b8ce869fc8b1c7bc483e8e4d7d1e899b1d5ebb68120b505d2e11 (the same command replayed on 2026-10-28, read only, complete lines). The replay equals the stored file on every character within the first 170 of every line, so every number reproduces. docs/PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARK.md section 12.4 recorded the input hashes but no output hash; the two output hashes above are recorded here for the first time.
- Result: Celeb-DF family test_x, real videos n=744: seed-mean FPR 0.1098 (per seed [0.0981, 0.121, 0.1102]). DFD test_x, real videos n=363: seed-mean FPR 0.1405 (per seed [0.1515, 0.1322, 0.1377]). For reference, the in-domain FaceForensics++ test_in block (real videos n=140) gives 0.0143. The values are copied from the script output, and they equal the cells of the rows of docs/PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARK.md marked for I-4.csv.
- Reading and limits: no bootstrap interval was computed (none was registered), so the rates describe these real-video sets and are not estimates for other video; the real videos come from few sources (Celeb-DF v2: 744 real videos; DFD: 28 groups), the threshold was chosen on FaceForensics++ validation data, and false-alarm behaviour on other kinds of real video is not measured here. They are the reason the face-video flag reads "Possible manipulation" with high uncertainty (D-087 addendum); the documents quote them only in the results and limitations, as "about 0.11" and "about 0.14", and cite this entry. The FakeAVCeleb specificity and sensitivity at the frozen threshold are E-015 and are not repeated here.
